# Branch dispositions — the ONLY branch-disposition authority for this repository

Created by phase GOV-3 on 2026-09-04 (OWNER_DECISION D-LEDGER, applied default). Before this file
existed, no record in the repository said what should happen to any branch; branch disposition
lived in one person's head and in one AI session's private memory. **This file is the authority.
Session memory is not.** Operating rules: `.claude/skills/thenijobs/references/merge.md` (rows,
claims, worktree lifecycle) and `references/promote.md` (the `## Promotions` table). Checked by
`scripts/governance/validate-branch-dispositions.mjs` (warnings only; read them).

## The rules

- **A branch absent from this file has NO disposition and MUST NOT be merged.** Absence is not
  neutral. Find out before merging it.
- **Only the owner changes a disposition.** A session may ADD a row for a branch it just created.
  A session may never CHANGE another branch's `Status` cell, not even to correct it. Flag and ask.
- **CLAIM BEFORE YOU START — the row is a lock, not a receipt.** See the claim protocol.
- **Phase branches merge into `develop`, never into `main`** (OWNER_DECISION D-BRANCH,
  2026-09-04). `staging` and `main` move only by fast-forward promotion with the owner's word in
  the same message, recorded in `## Promotions`. A merge commit or a direct commit on `staging` or
  `main` is a defect.
- **Nothing in this file authorizes a deploy.** `main` = production on Vercel (see the `main` row).
  The agent never runs `firebase deploy`, `vercel --prod`, or any equivalent.

## Claim protocol — read this before creating a branch

1. **Re-read the `## Branches` table.** Check for a row whose `Why` cell overlaps the work you are
   about to start, whatever its `Status`.
2. **If an overlapping row exists and is `ACTIVE`, STOP and ask the owner.** Two sessions on one
   scope is the failure mode, not a merge conflict to resolve later.
3. **Collision check by command, not memory:** `git worktree list` · `git branch --list
   'thenijobs/*'` · the `ACTIVE` rows below. A row on an unmerged branch is invisible from
   `develop`, so all three are mandatory.
4. **Add your row as the FIRST commit on the new branch**, `Status` = `ACTIVE`, `SHA` = the base
   commit, `Why` naming the files or directories you expect to touch and the collision check you
   ran. Commit it with a pathspec: `git commit -- docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md`.
5. **Then do the work.** Expand the `Why` cell with findings as you go; commit ledger edits
   immediately, never leave the ledger dirty.
6. **Committing directly to `main` is a defect under this model**, not a phase. If it ever happens,
   add a row naming the commit so the next session can see it.
7. On merge, the bookkeeping commit on `develop` sets `Status` = `MERGED_DEVELOP`, fills `SHA` with
   the branch tip, and `Merged into` with the merge commit. No placeholder may survive the merge.

## Status tokens

| Token | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `ACTIVE` | claimed, unmerged; the `SHA` cell is the base at claim time until the bookkeeping commit |
| `MERGED_DEVELOP` | contained in `develop`; `Merged into` = the `--no-ff` merge commit on `develop` |
| `ENVIRONMENT` | `develop`, `staging`, `main` themselves; moved only by merge (develop) or fast-forward promotion |
| `PRESERVED_REFERENCE` | holds commits absent from `develop`; **never merge without a new owner decision**; never delete |
| `SUPERSEDED_BY:<branch>` | replaced by another branch; kept as history |
| `ARCHIVED_TAG:<tag>` | tagged and retired |

## Branch model (OWNER_DECISION D-BRANCH, 2026-09-04, applied default — owner pushes)

`thenijobs/<phase>` → `--no-ff` merge into `develop` (inside the develop worktree) → fast-forward-only
promotion `develop` → `staging` → `main`, each promotion with the owner's word in the same message.
`main` never receives a merge commit or a direct commit. `develop` and `staging` were created at the
production tip `5b611114` by GOV-3 and exist locally until the owner pushes them.

Index deployment is the owner's (`firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes --project thenijobs-9f01d`), never the agent's. **Scope extended 2026-09-05, recorded before the change**, to `src/app/seeker/messages/page.tsx`: while confirming defect 1 I found the chat is broken in BOTH directions, not only the thread list. The two pages write and order by different timestamp fields — `applyToJob` and the seeker write `createdAt`, the employer writes `timestamp`; the seeker reads `orderBy('createdAt')`, the employer reads `orderBy('timestamp')`. Firestore `orderBy` excludes documents that lack the field, so a seeker never sees an employer reply and an employer never sees a seeker message or the system message. Fixing only the employer file would leave the feature half-broken, so both chat pages are standardised on `createdAt` and both sort client-side over `createdAt ?? timestamp`, which keeps existing messages of either shape visible without a data migration. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits `4514b55` (claim), `7a5386b` (scope), `cfa6945` (owner uid for threads and alerts), `169171d` (one chat time field, employer threads keyed on companyId), `59ce597` (candidate modal setter), `386b58b` (errors indexes). Two defects, both verified by reading the rule against the code that exercises it. **(1) A seeker cannot alert the employer.** `firestore.rules` notifications `create` allows only `isAdmin()`, an employer/owner creating for anyone, a create for one's own uid, or `userId == 'admin'`. `applyToJob` runs as the job seeker and addresses the employer's uid, so the write is denied the moment the rules ship — FIX-1 (`cfa6945`) fixed the address but could not fix the permission, because `firestore.rules` was outside that phase. Fix: a fifth create branch that lets a seeker notify a company owner **only about an application they themselves made**, proven by the `applications` document rather than asserted — the notification must carry `applicationId` and `companyId`, the application's `seekerId` must equal the caller, its `companyId` must match, and the named company's `ownerId` must equal the recipient. Field-presence is checked first so `&&` short-circuits before any `get()`, and the branch is placed last so the existing paths match earlier and never pay for the reads. **(2) Nobody can mark a notification as read.** The `update` rule allows `affectedKeys().hasOnly(['isRead', 'readAt'])`, but the application writes `read` — `createNotification` writes `read: false` (`firestoreService.ts:601`), `markNotificationRead` writes `{ read: true }` (`:614`), `markAllNotificationsRead` queries `where('read','==',false)` (`:620`), `NotificationContext` counts `!n.read`, and `payment/verify/route.ts:220` writes `read`. Nothing in the tree writes `isRead`. So every 'mark as read' is denied once the rules ship and the unread badge never clears. Fix: allow `read` alongside `isRead` and `readAt`. Expected files: `firestore.rules`, `src/lib/firebase/firestoreService.ts` (add `applicationId` to the employer alert payload), this ledger. It still fails closed and the emulator continues evaluating the remaining `||` branches, so nothing is permitted that should not be — but it makes legitimate denials surface as errors and would bite the anonymous-auth OTP path, which signs in before the user document exists. (2) **Production sends no security headers whatsoever**: no CSP, no HSTS, no `X-Content-Type-Options`, no `Referrer-Policy`, no `Permissions-Policy`. The block that exists in `firebase.json` applies only to the stale `thenijobs-9f01d.web.app` copy, which serves no custom domain — it has never protected the live site. Approach: the CSP ships as **`Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`**, not enforcing. An enforcing CSP written without an end-to-end run against a live site with real users could break Razorpay checkout, the Google sign-in popup, the Firebase channels and the Google Maps embeds in seven portfolio templates. Report-Only is risk-free, surfaces violations in the browser console, and the promotion path is documented in the file. The other five headers are enforced because each is measurably safe here: nothing in the tree uses geolocation, camera or microphone (grepped); `X-Frame-Options` is `SAMEORIGIN` rather than `DENY` because portfolio templates embed content and a stricter value buys nothing; HSTS carries no `includeSubDomains` and no `preload`, so it cannot strand a subdomain. The allowlist is derived from what the code actually reaches, not copied from `firebase.json`: `images.unsplash.com` (30 references in `sampleCompanies.ts`), `googleMapsEmbed` iframes in seven templates, `checkout.razorpay.com`, `lh3.googleusercontent.com` and `storage.googleapis.com` (from `next.config.ts` remotePatterns), and the Firebase identity, Firestore and storage origins. Consequences, in order: (1) `apiKey` can never be falsy, so the `503 service unavailable` branch immediately below it is dead code; (2) the request to `2factor.in` is therefore made with a **Firebase key as the SMS vendor's API key**, which both fails and deposits a live Google credential in a third party's access logs; (3) when that call fails, `data` keeps its optimistic initial value `{ Status: 'Success', Details: 'voice_session_<timestamp>' }`, so **the route answers `success` with a synthetic session id it invented**; (4) `src/app/api/otp/verify/route.ts:47` accepts `123456` or `999999` for **any** session id beginning `test_session_` or `voice_session_`, and the prefix is caller-supplied, so the id need not even come from step 3; (5) `src/app/login/page.tsx` then calls `signInAnonymously` and writes `isVerified: true` onto the new user document. End to end: **a caller could obtain a phone-verified account with the code 123456 without any call ever being placed.** Mitigating and stated plainly: all seven `src/app/api/**` routes return 404 in the production static export, and `PHONE_LOGIN_ENABLED` is `false` in the login page, so this is not exploitable against the live site today. It becomes exploitable the moment `D-HOSTING` is resolved in favour of running the routes. That is exactly why it is worth fixing now rather than later. Fixes: `otp/call` reads the environment variable only, so the existing 503 branch becomes live, and the optimistic fallback is removed so a failed vendor call reports a failure instead of inventing a session. `otp/verify` only honours the fixed test codes when **no** provider key is configured, and rejects caller-supplied test prefixes outright when a key is present; the separate `if (!apiKey) return verified: true` branch, which accepted any code at all, is replaced with an explicit unavailable response. `adminUserService.ts` stops duplicating the Firebase config with literal fallbacks and reuses the already-initialised default app's own options, which removes the duplication as well as the literal. `gate.sh`'s `secrets:src-literals` check currently excludes `adminUserService.ts` by name; with the literal gone the exclusion is removed, so the check becomes strictly stronger and **goes green for the first time** in this programme. (1) **`StatsSection.tsx` has zero importers.** `src/app/page.tsx` never renders it, so its three `useRealtimeCount` calls, including the duplicated companies query, do not execute on any page. The statement that the home page subscribes three times to draw counters is false. Loading the home page and searching for the 'Verified Pages' label found no such section. (2) **Four requests to `/Listen/channel` is not four subscriptions.** The Firestore SDK multiplexes every listener onto one webchannel and issues a handshake request followed by long-poll RPCs against the same endpoint, so the count of requests measures connection chatter, not the number of `onSnapshot` calls. Counting requests as subscriptions, which the 2026-09-05 audit did, overstates the problem. **What is actually true:** the only live subscription a signed-out visitor's home page creates is `WalkInDriveBanner`'s single `useCollection`, and `StatsSection` is dead code carrying a duplicated query. The revised scope is therefore smaller and still worth doing: convert the walk-in strip to a one-shot read, so the home page holds no persistent Firestore connection for a signed-out visitor, and delete the dead component rather than leaving a subscription-shaped trap for whoever wires it up next. Acceptance becomes: **zero `/Listen/channel` requests** on a cold home page, measured the same way before and after. **SECOND CORRECTION, after measuring: that acceptance criterion was also wrong.** A one-shot `getDocs` uses the *same* `/Listen/channel` endpoint as `onSnapshot` in the web SDK, so 'zero Listen requests' is unreachable and would not have meant anything. Counting requests, and then counting whether the request count grows over time, both failed to separate the two states: an idle long-poll does not reconnect inside a 30-second window, so the after-state and the reverted before-state both showed zero growth. **The metric that does separate them is how long a single request stays open.** A live `onSnapshot` parks a long-poll; a one-shot read completes in milliseconds. Measured in the browser against `next dev` in the phase worktree with real `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` values, same page, same method: **before** (the fix reverted in the working tree with `git revert --no-commit HEAD`, hot-reloaded) — 6 Firestore requests, one of them **held open for 45,586 ms**, 1 request open beyond 10 s; **after** — 5 Firestore requests, longest **81 ms**, **0** open beyond 10 s, at a comparable page age of 50 s. | `thenijobs/sec2-tighten-open-write-paths` | `989daf4` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEC-2 | The four write paths RULES-1 left open (`security.md` S-12). Every caller was inventoried first, because a rule the application itself violates is a broken feature, not a fix. **(1) `counters/{id}` is `read, write: if isAuthenticated()`.** Any signed-in account can rewrite the THENIJOBS ID sequence, so two businesses could be issued the same registration number, or the counter could be reset. The only writer is `identityService.ts` at `:26` and `:111`, both the same transaction: `transaction.set(counterRef, { count: previous + 1, updatedAt })`, creating with `count: 1` when the document is absent. That shape is expressible as a rule, so the rule can allow exactly it. **(2) `activityLogs` accepts a create from anyone signed in.** The admin audit trail can be poisoned with entries attributed to other people. All ten callers pass either the acting user's own uid or an admin's uid, so requiring `userId == request.auth.uid` (or `isAdmin()`) matches every real caller. **(3) `conversations` and `conversations/{id}/messages` accept a create with no participant check.** Any signed-in user can open a thread naming other people, or post a message into someone else's thread. Conversations are created only by `applyToJob`, which sets `seekerId` to the caller and includes them in `participants`. Messages are written by both chat pages with `senderId = user.uid`, plus one system message written by `applyToJob` with `senderId: 'system'`. The message rule must therefore mirror the existing READ rule — participant, or the seeker, or the company owner, which is how an employer reaches a pre-FIX-1 thread whose `participants` holds a company id — and accept `senderId` of either the caller or `'system'`. **(4) `leads` accepts an UNAUTHENTICATED create** with only a three-field presence check and no size limit: an open funnel into every company owner's inbox. Two findings while inventorying it: **nothing in the tree creates a lead** — `grep` finds reads, a count, an update and no create anywhere in `src/` — so today the rule is pure attack surface for a feature with no client. And the three shapes disagree: the rule demands `name`, `employer/leads/page.tsx` reads `customerName`, `admin/leads/page.tsx` and `lib/types` read `contactName`. A `hasOnly` allowlist would be inventing a schema, so this phase bounds the damage instead: keep the required fields, cap the free text, cap the field count, and require the named company to actually exist. | `thenijobs/sec3-remove-unused-rtdb` | `42edf1b` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEC-3 | **A second database nobody uses, downloaded by every visitor, and readable by the world.** `security.md` S-11. `src/lib/firebase/config.ts:6` imports `firebase/database`, `:29` calls `getDatabase(app)` at module scope so a Realtime Database client is constructed on every page load, and `:49` exports a `getFirebaseRtdb()` accessor. A repository-wide grep for `rtdb`, `getDatabase`, `getFirebaseRtdb` and `firebase/database` finds **no reference anywhere else in `src/`**. The client is created, its SDK ships in the shared chunk that all 463 pages load, and nothing ever reads or writes through it. Separately `database.rules.json` grants `".read": true` on three node trees — `companies`, `jobs` and `reviews`. A read-only probe on 2026-09-05 confirmed an anonymous shallow read of `/companies.json` on the live instance returns **200**, while `/users.json` correctly returns 401. So the rules are not theoretical: the instance answers unauthenticated reads today. It happens to hold nothing, because the app never wrote to it — but the tree is open, and 'empty' is a property of the data, not of the rules. Fix: delete the import, the `getDatabase` call and the accessor, and rewrite `database.rules.json` to deny by default. Both halves matter and neither substitutes for the other: removing the client stops shipping the SDK, and closing the rules stops the instance answering strangers whether or not any client exists. Expected files: `src/lib/firebase/config.ts`, `database.rules.json`, this ledger. | `thenijobs/seo2-doubled-page-titles` | `6cae8f8` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEO-2 | **362 of the 464 exported pages ship a `<title>` that ends `| THENIJOBS | THENIJOBS`.** Measured from the built export at `1383c9f`, not estimated: 78% of the site. Live on production today — `https://www.thenijobs.com/company/<any-slug>` returns `Company Profile | THENIJOBS | THENIJOBS` and the 404 page returns `Page Not Found | THENIJOBS | THENIJOBS`. Google truncates around 60 characters, so a wasted repeat of twelve characters costs real words on every company profile, every category page and every job-location page. **Cause, and it is not a typo repeated 362 times.** `src/app/layout.tsx:28` sets `title.template = "%s | THENIJOBS"`, and most page titles already end with the same suffix. But it only doubles on *some* routes, which is the part worth writing down: `metadata.title` is a single field, so a nested layout that sets `title` to a plain string **replaces the whole inherited object, template included**, and its children then get no template at all. `src/app/jobs/layout.tsx` and `src/app/businesses/layout.tsx` do exactly that — which is why `/jobs` doubles while `/jobs/demo` does not, and why `/businesses` doubles while `/businesses/all` does not. Two conventions coexist in one codebase and neither is visible at the point of use. Fix: **delete the template and let every title stand on its own.** The alternative — keep the template and strip the suffix from the titles that double — cannot be applied uniformly, because the titles under `/jobs/**` and `/businesses/**` receive no template and would lose their suffix entirely. Keeping it means the correct way to write a title depends on whether some ancestor happens to set one, which is the interaction that produced the bug. `title.default` stays, so every page that sets no title of its own is unaffected. Expected files: `src/app/layout.tsx`, plus whichever titles turn out to rely on the template for their only suffix — determined by rebuilding and diffing all 464 titles, not by guessing — and this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `vercel.json`, `package.json`, `next.config.ts`, or any client component. The production pages only change when the owner deploys. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `bc7af28`, then `6cae8f8`. The real prices are `SUBSCRIPTION_PLANS` in `src/lib/constants.ts` — free 0, **standard 480, premium 1200, enterprise 5000** — and `PaymentCheckoutModal.tsx:182` sends `amount: plan.price` from exactly that list. Please contact support."** for every paid plan. `basic` is not even a plan any more; the comment above the table says "must match pricing page" and it does not. **(2) Blocking one script grants a paid subscription.** `PaymentCheckoutModal.tsx:38-50` resolves `false` when `checkout.razorpay.com` fails to load — an ad blocker, a corporate proxy, DevTools request blocking, or no network. `:169-175` then takes an else-branch that calls `handleVerifyPayment(orderId, 'pay_direct_' + Date.now(), 'direct_authorized')`: **the client fabricates a payment id and a signature and asks the server to activate the subscription, with no money involved.** The server accepts it, because `verify/route.ts:73` only verifies the signature `if (razorpaySecret && paymentId && signature)` — with `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` unset it skips verification entirely and proceeds to write an active one-year subscription and set `isPremium: true`. | `thenijobs/seo4-sitemap-lastmod-lies` | `d5a5bee` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEO-4 | **173 of the 384 sitemap entries claim to have been modified at the moment the site was built.** Measured from the built `sitemap.xml`: 384 entries, every one carries a `<lastmod>`, and **173 of them share the single value `2026-09-05T03:02:55.925Z`** — the build clock. Google's own guidance is that it stops trusting `lastmod` when it does not match reality, which means the value is not merely useless here — it degrades the signal for the **211 entries that are telling the truth**, the jobs, companies and portfolios carrying a real `updatedAt`. Surfaced by DEP-2: comparing two builds a minute apart showed **346 differing lines in `sitemap.xml`** while nothing in the repository had changed. The same file also has a quieter version of the fault. Each dynamic entry does `let lastMod = now;` and overwrites it only when `updatedAt` parses, so a job or company with a missing or malformed `updatedAt` silently claims the build time too. None did in this build, which is exactly why it needs fixing rather than watching. Fix: **omit `lastModified` where there is no real signal.** It is optional in the sitemap protocol and in `MetadataRoute.Sitemap`, and no date is honest where a wrong one is not. Static and generated-listing URLs lose it; jobs, companies and portfolios keep it **only when their own `updatedAt` parses**. Kept by jobs, companies on **both** URL forms, and portfolios, all of which carry a genuine `updatedAt`. **Revert-and-watch:** reverting `d5a5bee` and building twice reproduces **346 differing lines**, the same figure DEP-2 arrived at independently while comparing two of its own builds. | `thenijobs/perf4-firestore-persistent-cache` | `658acd9` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("the app should be blazing fast … with a proper cache system", 2026-09-05; and "start fixing all these bugs one by one") via phase PERF-4 | **Every visit pays a fresh network round trip before any content appears, because Firestore is running with no cache at all.** `src/lib/firebase/config.ts:26` is `getFirestore(app)`, which gives the **memory-only** cache: it is emptied on every page load, so nothing is ever reused between visits or sessions. Measured on a fresh `/jobs` load: DOM ready at **83 ms**, and the last Firestore response arriving at **414 ms** — the page has nothing to show for the 331 ms in between, on localhost, against a dataset of three jobs. On a phone in Theni talking to a Google region, that gap is the page. This is the cache layer the performance plan called the biggest user-visible win, and it is the last item of that plan still undone. Layer 0 (headers, image weight) shipped as PERF-0, query discipline as PERF-2 and PERF-3, and the home page's parked subscriptions as PERF-1. Fix: initialise Firestore with `persistentLocalCache`, so documents already fetched live in IndexedDB across loads and sessions and a returning visitor renders from disk while the SDK revalidates in the background. That is stale-while-revalidate for Firestore, obtained from the SDK rather than hand-rolled. **Three things this must not get wrong.** (1) `initializeFirestore` has to run **before** anything calls `getFirestore`, and exactly once — `config.ts` is the only place that constructs it, which is what makes this safe. (2) There is **no IndexedDB during the build**, where Next collects page data in Node, so persistence is browser-only and the server path keeps the plain instance. (3) Persistence **can legitimately fail** — private browsing, blocked site data, an unsupported browser — and a failure there must degrade to the current behaviour rather than break the app, so it falls back to `getFirestore(app)`. **`@tanstack/react-query` is deliberately not reinstated.** DEP-2 removed it unused and said re-adding was one command. It is not needed: the SDK's own persistent cache does this for every read in the app, with no provider to thread through 46 `useCollection` call sites and 24 `getDocs` ones, and **no bundle growth**. A library that duplicates a cache the SDK already ships is not a cache system, it is a second one. Expected files: `src/lib/firebase/config.ts`, this ledger. **No page or component changes** — if a page needs editing to benefit, the change is in the wrong place. Never touches `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `package.json`, `vercel.json`, `next.config.ts`. Lanes: worker, skill. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`0729420`) + this one; the second session's worktree is **gone** and its branch fully merged; `ACTIVE` ledger rows on `develop` = **0**. Worktree: `/Users/saai_siddharth/Projects/Clients/Theni-Jobs-perf4`. Acceptance: **measured in a real browser, same page, same server, before and after** — a repeat load of `/jobs` must render its cards without waiting on a Firestore response, quoted as a number rather than described; the **first** load must be no worse; `persistentLocalCache` must be provably in use, not merely requested, by observing the IndexedDB database it creates; the build must still pass with **no IndexedDB present**, which is the case that would break it; typecheck 0, build 0 with real credentials, lint no worse than the 2-error baseline, export page count unchanged at 569. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `42bc30d`, then `658acd9`. Gate after the last commit: typecheck 0 · lint 1 (**2 errors / 685 warnings, the baseline**) · **build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes** · secrets:bundle 0 · secrets:src-literals 0 · tracked-artefacts 0 · dirty-after 0. Export unchanged at **569 pages**, which is also the proof for hazard (2): the build collects page data in Node where there is no IndexedDB, and it passes. **Proven by looking at what is actually on disk, not by trusting the config.** With the change, IndexedDB contains **`firestore/[DEFAULT]/thenijobs-9f01d/main`**, whose `remoteDocumentsV14` store holds documents from the **`jobs`** collection and whose `targets` store holds the **`jobs`** query — the /jobs page's own data, persisted across loads. Without the change, after the identical load, **no `firestore/` database exists at all**. Site data was wiped before each run so both were genuinely cold, and both rendered the same three cards, so nothing regressed. **Baseline that motivated it:** a cold `/jobs` load had `domContentLoadedEventEnd` at **83 ms** and the last Firestore response at **414 ms** — on localhost, against three job documents. The page had nothing to show for the 331 ms in between. **Not measured, and it matters that this is said rather than glossed: a warm-load latency figure.** The browser pane exposes **no `paint` and no `largest-contentful-paint` entries**, and a script injected after load starts too late to observe first render — one attempt returned `cardsPresentAtMs: 8782`, which is the age of the document and not a load time. Any millisecond figure for the warm case would therefore have been invented. What is established is that the cache is **enabled**, is **populated with this page's own documents**, and **does not exist without this change**; the rendering benefit follows from the SDK's documented behaviour of serving listeners from cache before the server responds, which is a citation and not a measurement. **`@tanstack/react-query` deliberately not reinstated.** DEP-2 removed it unused and recorded that re-adding was one command. It is not needed: the SDK's own cache covers **all 46 `useCollection` call sites and all 24 `getDocs` ones** with no provider to thread through them and **no bundle growth**. A library duplicating a cache the SDK already ships is not a cache system, it is a second one. **Three hazards, each answered by a line in the file rather than by hope.** (1) `initializeFirestore` must precede any `getFirestore` and run once — this module is the sole constructor and `db` is what the app imports, so the ordering holds by construction. (2) No IndexedDB in Node, so the server path keeps the plain instance. (3) Persistence can be refused — private browsing, blocked site data — and that costs the cache, never Firestore, so it falls back to precisely the previous behaviour. `persistentMultipleTabManager` avoids the first tab taking an exclusive lease and later ones silently getting no persistence. **This closes the performance plan.** Layer 0 shipped as PERF-0 (headers, 1.4 MB of images), query discipline as PERF-2 and PERF-3, the home page's parked subscriptions as PERF-1, and the bundle as SEC-3's 106 KB. The data cache was the last item and the one the owner asked for twice. Lanes: worker PASS, skill PASS. **Findings carried out:** (a) Stale-while-revalidate means a returning visitor can see a job that has since closed, for the moment before revalidation lands. That is the correct trade for a job board and it is the trade being made — worth knowing before anyone reports it as a bug. (b) The cache is unbounded by default; Firestore garbage-collects at a 40 MB target, which is fine now and worth revisiting if the job or company collections grow by an order of magnitude. (c) The 24 one-shot `getDocs` call sites are **server-first** by design, so they benefit from persistence only for offline and for listener-backed screens; making those cache-first would be a per-call-site change and a separate phase, and should be measured before it is assumed to be worth it. | `b759b44` (2026-09-05, inside `Theni-Jobs-develop`) |

Adding `isCompanyOwner(resource.data.companyId)` to the jobs read rule makes the **existing queries pass unchanged** — proven — and needs **no query edits and no new composite indexes**, where adding `where('postedBy','==',uid)` to three screens would have needed both, and would have hidden a colleague's postings from a company's other users the moment one existed. Placed after the two free comparisons and before `isAdmin()`, so the common paths short-circuit before either `get()` — the ordering lesson RULES-2 learned the hard way. Expected files: `firestore.rules`, this ledger. **No source, no indexes** — if a page needs editing, the fix is in the wrong place. Never touches `storage.rules`, `database.rules.json`, `firestore.indexes.json`, `src/**`. Acceptance: an **emulator probe with real signed-in users** in which the employer's existing queries are **DENIED on the current rules and ALLOWED on the new ones**, a **different account is denied on both**, and the seeded data is created through the real create rules rather than injected past them. The probe must be **idempotent** — unique ids per run, the lesson from RULES-2. Rules reach production only on the owner's `firebase deploy`. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `a9bbcd6`, then `4f28c40`. Gate: typecheck 0 · lint 1 (**2 errors / 685 warnings, baseline**) · **build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes** · secrets 0 · artefacts 0 · dirty-after 0. **Probe, both rule sets, real signed-in users, ids unique per run, data seeded through the real create rules rather than injected past them:** `employer/jobs` (`companyId` + `orderBy createdAt`) **DENY → ALLOW**; `employer/dashboard` (`companyId` + `isActive`) **DENY → ALLOW**; a different account listing those jobs **DENY on both**; a query with no status constraint **DENY on both**; `status == 'active'` **ALLOW on both**. Two assertions deny under both sets, so a run that loaded no rules could not have passed. **Why the rule moved and not the queries.** Adding `where('postedBy','==',uid)` to the three screens would have worked, and would have cost two new composite indexes and hidden a colleague's postings from a company's other users the day a second one existed. The queries were written for the policy the rest of the file uses; the rule was the outlier. **Ordering:** `isCompanyOwner()` sits after the two free comparisons and before `isAdmin()`, so the common paths short-circuit before either `get()` — the lesson RULES-2 learned when hoisting `isAdmin()` turned clean refusals into null-value evaluation errors. **Two assumptions of mine the probe corrected**, both the rules working as intended and both recorded rather than quietly fixed: creating a job with `status: 'active'` is denied because `safeJobCreate()` permits only `pending` and `draft` — a poster cannot self-activate, which is D-JOBSTATE; and creating a company with `isActive: true` is denied by `safeCompanyCreate()` for the same reason. The fallback has been standing in front of correct UI. **I own the scale of this.** SEO-3 took this family from 30 pages to 135 on the grounds that the deployed sitemap already promised them and the implementation was location-agnostic. That check is the one this phase adds. Fix: delete the clause that makes every Theni job match every location, and delete the fallback so the empty state that exists becomes reachable. Expected files: `src/components/seo/CategoryJobPageClient.tsx`, `src/components/seo/LocationJobPageClient.tsx`, this ledger. No route, sitemap or rules change. The hero renders them as "500+ Active Jobs · 190+ Verified Companies · 1,200+ Job Seekers". **Measured against production on 2026-09-05, read-only aggregation queries:** `jobs where status == 'active'` = **2**. `companies where verificationStatus == 'verified'` = **104** (and 104 companies in total). So the headline claim overstates by **250×**, and the company claim by about 83%. The seeker figure cannot be checked at all without reading `users`, which the rules correctly restrict to the owner and admins. This is the same defect class as TRUST-1, which removed three invented businesses, and it is more prominent: it is the first thing a job seeker sees on a live site with real users, presented as measured fact. **Four more places say the same kind of thing.** `src/components/auth/AuthShell.tsx:21` — `['1,200+ Active Jobs', '500+ Verified Companies', '98% Placement Rate']`, shown on the login screen; a placement rate is not merely wrong here, it is unverifiable and the strongest claim on the site. `src/app/about/page.tsx:100-103` — `10,000+ Active Users`, `1,500+ Verified Companies`, `5,000+ Connections Made`, `15+ Cities Served`, against 104 companies and **9** locations in `LOCATIONS_DATA`. `src/app/jobs/layout.tsx:6` puts "Search 1,200+ verified private jobs" in the **meta description**, so it reaches search results. `src/app/seeker/subscription/page.tsx:53` sells a paid plan on "stand out to 500+ verified employers" — a number attached to a payment. Fix, and the line is deliberate: **count what can be counted, say nothing where it cannot.** The hero takes real counts through `getCountFromServer` for `jobs where status == 'active'` and `companies where verificationStatus == 'verified'` — both provably readable by an anonymous visitor under the current rules — and **drops the job-seeker figure entirely**, because counting `users` would need permissions no visitor should have. The `+` goes with it: a plus sign on an exact count is its own small untruth. Everywhere a number cannot be counted, qualitative wording replaces it — the pattern `register/page.tsx:117` already uses, `['Free for job seekers', 'Verified local businesses', 'Setup in minutes']`, which is honest and reads better. Expected files: `src/components/home/HeroSection.tsx`, `src/components/auth/AuthShell.tsx`, `src/app/about/page.tsx`, `src/app/jobs/layout.tsx`, `src/app/seeker/subscription/page.tsx`, this ledger. Acceptance: **no fabricated quantity survives anywhere in the tree**, checked by grep for `NNN+` claim strings; the hero shows the **real** counts in a browser against production data — 2 and 104 on the day of writing, not 500 and 190; the counts come from aggregation queries an anonymous visitor is allowed to run, proven by loading the page **signed out**; typecheck 0, build 0 with real credentials, lint no worse than the 2-error baseline, page count unchanged at 569. **Note for the owner rather than a decision taken here:** the honest number is small today and will grow. If the front page should not show a live count while it is small, the fix is to show no count — not to show a made-up one. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `544ab29`, then `cc8dfe6`. Gate: typecheck 0 · lint 1 (**2 errors / 683 warnings, baseline**) · **build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes** · secrets 0 · artefacts 0 · dirty-after 0 · **569 pages**. **Verified in a browser, signed out** — which is how the page is usually seen, and the state in which the counts have to be permitted: the hero renders **"2 Active Jobs · 104 Verified Companies"**. No `500+`, no `190+`, no `1,200+`, and no Job Seekers card. The counts loading at all for an anonymous visitor is the proof that each aggregation query carries the constraint its read rule needs. **How it was found:** by opening the site. Twenty-seven phases of reading this code had not surfaced it, because nothing about `useState(500)` is a *bug* to a compiler, a linter or a security probe — it is a lie to a person, and only a person looking at the page can see it. Two phases in a row have now been found this way. **The line applied throughout: count what can be counted, say nothing where it cannot.** Jobs and companies are counted with `getCountFromServer`, which bills one read per thousand documents rather than reading them. | `thenijobs/trust3-generated-sites-assert-unverified-claims` | `c6a6ac5` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-3 | **The generated "Official Website" for each real business asserts credentials, availability and opening hours the business never supplied.** Found by opening one: `/uthayam-air-travels`, a **travel agency**, is advertised with **"On-Demand Repairs & Trusted Service Guarantee"**, **"Express Service & Technician Booking"**, **"On-site Visits • Certified Technicians • Service Warranty"**, careers headed **"Electrician, Plumber & Technician Jobs"**, and an FAQ stating as fact that it is **"open Monday through Saturday from 9:00 AM to 8:00 PM"**. The page is badged **"Verified Official Website · Powered by THENIJOBS"**, so a visitor reads all of it as the business's own words. **Three causes, all in `src/components/company/CompanyLandingWebsite.tsx`.** **(1) The preset matcher picks the wrong industry.** `:231` is `catLower.includes(m.toLowerCase())` over `Object.keys(INDUSTRY_THEMES)` **in declaration order**. The `service-business` preset lists the bare token `'Services'` and is declared before `corporate-professional`, so the category *"Professional & Business Services"* matches `'Services'` first and a travel agency is dressed as a repair shop. Any category containing a generic word is captured by whichever preset claims that word earliest. **(2) Every preset asserts specifics whichever one is chosen.** `healthcare-hospital` announces **"24/7 Medical Care"** — a clinic open nine-to-five advertised as always available, which is the kind of claim someone acts on in an emergency. `manufacturing-industry` announces **"ISO Certified Production"**, a certification the business may not hold. `education-college` claims **"accredited degree programs"**, `corporate-professional` **"certified financial auditing"**, `service-business` a **"verified warranty on all repair services"**. These are regulated, checkable claims made on behalf of named third parties by a directory they did not write. **(3) The hours are invented outright.** `:278` answers "What are the operating hours?" with `company.businessHours || '...open Monday through Saturday from 9:00 AM to 8:00 PM'`, and `:1191` prints the same fallback in the location panel. Where the business supplied nothing, the page states hours anyway — someone can travel to a shop that is shut because this page said it was open. This completes a family: TRUST-1 removed **invented businesses**, TRUST-2 removed **invented statistics**, this removes **invented claims about real businesses** — the most exposed of the three, because each one names a real company. Fix: make the matcher prefer the most specific category token rather than the first declared; replace every badge and tagline asserting a credential, certification or availability with wording describing what the directory actually knows; and state opening hours **only** when the business supplied them. | `thenijobs/seo6-category-match-precision` | `d822f9f` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEO-6 | **Three matching-precision bugs in the location/category job pages, found while scoping and confirmed against live data and unit tests rather than eyeballed.** **(1) A two-letter category slug matched inside unrelated words.** `CategoryJobPageClient.tsx`'s `matchCat` used `.toLowerCase().includes(categorySlug)` against `title`/`category`/`jobType`. Checked programmatically against the real 15-slug category list: `'it'` is a substring of `'hospitality'` and of `'security'`, and of the common job word `'kitchen'`. A Hospitality, Security or Kitchen job could be shown on the **IT & Software** category page. Side-by-side, old vs new, on the same four titles: `Hospitality Manager` **true -> false**, `Security Guard` **true -> false**, `Kitchen Staff` **true -> false**, `IT Support Executive` **true -> true** — the one genuine match survives, the three accidents do not. Fixed with a word-boundary regex, which is **strictly a subset** of what `.includes()` matched — anything that matched as a whole word before still matches; only mid-word accidents are excluded. It does not add recall (a title with no shared substring, like 'Driver Required' against slug `driving`, still will not match) — that is a synonym-expansion problem, the kind `/jobs/page.tsx` solves with `RULE_BASED_SYNONYMS`, and a different, larger fix than the false-positive bug closed here. **(2) `full-time`/`part-time` could never match by jobType at all.** Read directly from production: both real job documents store `jobType: 'full_time'` — underscore. The slugs are `'full-time'`/`'part-time'` — hyphen. `.includes('full-time')` against `'full_time'` never matches, confirmed live, so `/jobs-in-*/full-time` and `/jobs-in-*/part-time` could only ever show a job by an exact literal hyphen appearing in its title, which no real title has. Fixed by comparing on the normalised form. **(3) `work-from-home` shares no substring with its real value at all.** The app's own jobType vocabulary (`src/app/jobs/page.tsx:371-372`) stores this as `'remote'` or `'wfh'` — words with nothing in common with the slug. No hyphen/underscore swap fixes that, so an explicit two-entry alias map closes it, grounded in the vocabulary the rest of the app already uses rather than invented. **(4) Both components fetch 15 jobs and filter client-side, so a genuine match past the 15th was silently dropped.** Raised to `PUBLIC_LIST_LIMIT = 500`, the same cap and the same name PERF-3 set on `/jobs` and `/businesses` for the identical reason. **Deliberately not done, and said so rather than left silent:** unlike `/jobs` and `/businesses`, these two components do not surface a 'showing the first 500' notice when the cap is hit. At the current job volume the cap cannot bind, and adding pagination-style chrome to a landing page whose job is to funnel traffic to `/jobs` is scope beyond fixing the three matching bugs above; recorded as a gap rather than silently matched to PERF-3's UI as well as its limit. Also not done: multi-word slugs whose only difference from the real field is a space vs. a hyphen (`'customer service'` vs `'customer-service'`) still do not match — the same limitation the old code had, not a regression, and out of scope for a precision fix. Expected files: `src/components/seo/CategoryJobPageClient.tsx`, `src/components/seo/LocationJobPageClient.tsx`, this ledger. The app's own client, `PublicPortfolioPageClient.tsx:106`, checks `site.status !== 'published'` and shows 'This portfolio website is currently in draft mode' instead of the content — but that check runs in the browser, after the data has already been sent. A request that skips the app entirely — a REST call, a browser devtools query, anything that is not this specific React component — receives the full draft. **The static export is not the exposure.** `getAllPublishedPortfolioUsernamesServer()` (`firestoreServer.ts:378-390`) filters `status == 'published'` in the query itself at build time, so no draft is ever baked into a static HTML page. The gap is purely at the database layer: anyone who queries `portfolioSites` directly, bypassing the app's UI and its build-time filtering both, reads what the owner has not chosen to publish. Fix: `allow read` requires `status == 'published'`, or the requester is authenticated as the site's own `ownerId`, or an admin — the same shape every other status-gated collection in this file already uses (`companies`, `jobs`), and compatible with the one legitimate authenticated case this app has, the owner previewing their own unpublished site (`isDraftPreview` in `CompanyLandingWebsite.tsx`). No unauthenticated draft-preview-link feature exists to break — checked before assuming. Expected files: `firestore.rules`, this ledger. Never touches `storage.rules`, `database.rules.json`, `src/**`. Lanes: security, rules, skill. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`5a96c02`) + this one; `ACTIVE` rows = 0. Worktree: `/Users/saai_siddharth/Projects/Clients/Theni-Jobs-rules5`. Acceptance: an **emulator probe with real signed-in users** proving a draft site is denied to a stranger and to a signed-out request, allowed to its owner and to an admin, and a published site stays readable by anyone; **revert-and-watch** showing the old rule allowing exactly what the new one denies; at least one assertion denied under **both** rule sets. Rules reach production only on the owner's `firebase deploy`. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `b992dd9`, then `734fc24`. Gate: typecheck 0 · lint 1 (**2 errors / 683 warnings, baseline**) · **build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes** · secrets 0 · artefacts 0 · dirty-after 0. **Probe, both rule sets, real signed-in users, ids unique per run, data seeded through the real create rule:** a stranger reading a draft site goes **ALLOW -> DENY**; the owner, an admin, and anyone reading a published site all stay **ALLOW**; two write-side guards **DENY under both rule sets**. **A probe-design mistake, corrected before it was trusted.** The first version of this probe put its non-vacuity guard on the READ side — 'a signed-out client reads the draft, want DENY' — and it **failed under the old rules**, correctly: `allow read: if true` denies nothing at all on the read path, which is the entire finding this phase closes. A guard needs a case that was already gated before the phase touched anything, and the read side had none to offer. Moved to the write side, where create/update already required `ownerId` to match in both rule sets, unchanged by this phase. **Building the guard on the wrong side would have made a real vulnerability look like a broken probe** — worth keeping as a general lesson: a rule this permissive cannot supply its own read-side guard. **Discovered while sweeping for new bugs after the queue emptied of unblocked work**, not from a planned audit item — found by re-checking a rule comment ("filtering by status handled in query/client") against the pattern RULES-2 and RULES-3 had already named as the thing to distrust in this file. Lanes: security PASS, rules PASS, skill PASS. **Not verified:** no production probe — demonstrating the read against `thenijobs-9f01d` directly would mean reading a real business's actual unpublished content, which is exactly the thing being closed. The single read-only REST check that opened this phase already showed the live exposure without printing the draft's own content, and the emulator with real users stands in for the rest. **Owner action required, and urgent relative to the other rules already queued:** `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project thenijobs-9f01d` closes this alongside RULES-1 through RULES-4, all still undeployed. Until then, any business that has started building its website but not yet published it has that draft fully readable by anyone who asks. | `4d64c81` (2026-09-05, inside `Theni-Jobs-develop`) |
| `thenijobs/rules6-company-follows-and-dead-collections` | `0301812` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase RULES-6 | **A full inventory of every read rule in `firestore.rules`, following RULES-5's discovery that this file had more than one instance of the same anti-pattern.** Seven collections read `if true`: `reviews`, `services`, `advertisements`, `usernames`, `companyFollows`, `seoAnalytics`, `companySlugs`. Five are public-by-design and correct — reviews, services, ads and slugs are meant to be discoverable, and `companySlugs`'s public read is a **recorded owner decision (D-SLUG, RULES-1)** for duplicate-slug detection, not touched here even though a related concern about it is logged separately below. Two are not. **(1) `companyFollows` is the one user-relationship collection in this file that is not scoped to its own user.** `savedJobs`, `jobAlerts`, `notifications` and `subscriptions` all gate read with `resource.data.userId == request.auth.uid || isAdmin()`. `companyFollows` alone reads `if true`, so any stranger can enumerate exactly which real user follows which real company — a behavioural fact about a specific person, openly readable by anyone with no ownership check at all. Confirmed live: one real `{companyId, createdAt, userId}` document exists in production today, and it was read back with a plain unauthenticated request. Every one of the four call sites in `firestoreService.ts` (`followCompany`, `unfollowCompany`, `isFollowingCompany`) already queries by the caller's own `userId`, and the one UI caller (`JobDetailPageClient.tsx:267`) only runs when a `uid` is present — so scoping read to the owner changes nothing a signed-in user can already do and closes what a stranger could. **(2) `usernames` and `seoAnalytics` have zero references anywhere in `src/`** — no read, no write, in either direction. The same shape RULES-2 found in `whatsappApplications` and RULES-3 found in `certificates`/`uploads`: a rule block written for a feature that was never built, left exactly as open as the day it was drafted because nothing exercises it and nothing complains. Closed to default-deny, which is provably behaviour-preserving since nothing in the tree depends on the current openness. **Recorded, not fixed: `companySlugs` stores a business owner's plaintext email inline** in a document whose read rule the owner already decided should be public, for slug-duplicate checking. The stated purpose only needs slug *existence* to be checkable; the current implementation also lets anyone enumerate registered owners' email addresses by paginating the collection or querying `where('email','==',...)` directly — the exact query `register-business/page.tsx:99` runs client-side. This is not touched here: it sits inside a decision the owner already made (D-SLUG), and reversing part of a recorded decision without being asked is a different kind of change than closing an unexamined default. Logged as its own item for the owner rather than silently altered. Fix: `companyFollows` read scoped to the follower or an admin; `usernames` and `seoAnalytics` default-denied entirely. Expected files: `firestore.rules`, this ledger. Never touches `storage.rules`, `database.rules.json`, `src/**`, and does not touch `companySlugs`. Lanes: security, rules, skill. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`a55247b`) + this one; `ACTIVE` rows = 0. Worktree: `/Users/saai_siddharth/Projects/Clients/Theni-Jobs-rules6`. Acceptance: an **emulator probe with real signed-in users** proving a stranger cannot read another user's follow record while the follower and an admin still can; `usernames`/`seoAnalytics` denied to everyone including their own would-be creator, since nothing in the app ever creates one; **revert-and-watch** with the old rules allowing what the new ones deny; guards denied under both rule sets placed on the write side, following the lesson RULES-5 recorded about read-side guards being meaningless against an `if true` rule. Rules reach production only on the owner's `firebase deploy`. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `669f917`, then `0301812`. Gate: typecheck 0 · lint 1 (**2 errors / 683 warnings, baseline**) · **build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes** · secrets 0 · artefacts 0 · dirty-after 0. **Probe, both rule sets, real signed-in users, ids unique per run:** stranger reading another user's `companyFollows` record **ALLOW -> DENY**; the follower and an admin unchanged at **ALLOW**; a plain signed-in user reading `usernames` or `seoAnalytics` **ALLOW -> DENY**; an admin reading `seoAnalytics` unchanged at **ALLOW**, proving the admin-only closure rather than a full one; two write-side guards **DENY under both rule sets**. **A methodology note carried forward from RULES-5 and confirmed useful again:** both non-vacuity guards were placed on the write side deliberately — this file's `if true` reads deny nothing at all, so a read-side guard against an unconditionally-open rule proves nothing about the probe's validity. **The one item explicitly not touched is worth restating plainly.** `companySlugs` stores a business owner's plaintext email inline, in a document whose public read is `RULES-1`'s own recorded decision (`D-SLUG`) — and that decision's own comment names `email` as one of the stored fields, which is evidence the tradeoff was considered rather than an oversight. This phase logs, rather than reverses, that the implementation lets anyone enumerate registered owners' emails by paginating the collection or by the exact `where('email','==',...)` query `register-business/page.tsx` already runs client-side. A later phase or the owner directly can decide whether the stated purpose — checking whether a *slug* is taken — actually requires the *email* to travel in the same publicly-readable document. The read-only single-document check that opened this phase already showed the live exposure without printing anyone's identity. **Owner action:** this rules file now has six phases queued behind one `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project thenijobs-9f01d` — RULES-1 through RULES-6 — none of them live yet. | `f654ba9` (2026-09-05, inside `Theni-Jobs-develop`) |
| `thenijobs/pay2-job-posting-limit-not-enforced` | `14bf218` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase PAY-2 | **The Free plan's "1 Active Job Posting" limit, and every paid tier's higher limit, is decorative — nothing in the app stops an employer from posting past it.** `employer/post-job/page.tsx:82` computes `planCheck = canPostNewJob(planSlug, activeJobsCount)`, and `planCheck.allowed` drives exactly one thing: which banner renders at the top of the page — an amber "Job Posting Limit Reached" warning with an "Upgrade Plan" link when `false`, a plain blue counter when `true`. **`planCheck.allowed` is referenced nowhere else in the file.** The submit button's `disabled` prop (`:759`) is `loading || (step === 1 && !isStep1Valid)` — no reference to the plan check at all. `handlePost()` (`:141-160`) checks `!companyId` and an empty title, and nothing else, before calling `createDocument('jobs', jobPayload)`. **An employer who has already hit their plan's limit can read the warning banner, click through all four wizard steps, and successfully create another job.** The banner tells the truth about the limit; the button and the handler do not act on it. `firestore.rules`'s `safeJobCreate()` does not enforce a count either — it checks `status`, `isActive`, `isFeatured` and `rejectionReason`, never how many jobs the poster already has. A Firestore rule cannot cheaply count existing documents against a request without a `get()`/aggregation the way this file avoids elsewhere for cost reasons, so the natural enforcement point is the one this phase fixes, not the rule. **This is the only count-based plan limit found anywhere in the app** — a search for every file referencing `canPostNewJob`, `PLAN_FEATURE_MATRIX` or `hasFeaturePermission` finds exactly two: this page, and `employer/company-profile/page.tsx`, which only uses `hasFeaturePermission` for **boolean** feature gates (whether a whole tab is shown) rather than a numeric count. Whether other numeric limits named in `SUBSCRIPTION_PLANS.features` strings — 3 products, 6 gallery images, 5 team members — are enforced anywhere they are actually created is a separate question this phase does not answer; recorded as a finding rather than assumed either way. Fix: `handlePost()` refuses with the same early-return shape as its existing `!companyId` and empty-title guards when `!planCheck.allowed`; the submit button additionally disables on step 4 when `!planCheck.allowed`, so the flow visibly stops at the point that actually creates the job rather than after the fact. Steps 1-3 are left open — the warning banner already runs the whole time, and blocking earlier prevents nothing a blocked step 4 does not already prevent. Expected files: `src/app/employer/post-job/page.tsx`, this ledger. `src/lib/plans.ts`'s `canPostNewJob` itself is correct and untouched — the bug is that its result was computed and displayed but never acted on. The comparison that matters — before and after, same worktree, same commit — passed; the number quoted at claim time did not. **Not claimed:** an authenticated end-to-end click-through, which would require a real employer account already at their plan's limit and would write to `thenijobs-9f01d`. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `6f2b2d7`, ledger self-correction `74714af`, fix `14bf218`, merge `b62cacc`. | `thenijobs/trust6-broadcast-open-rate-is-fabricated` | `299d912` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-6 | **An admin sending a push/SMS/email broadcast writes an invented open rate into Firestore, indistinguishable from a real one.** Found by a repository-wide grep for TODO/FIXME/XXX/HACK markers, which surfaced a self-documented `FIXME` already left in the file by an earlier phase. `src/app/admin/notifications/page.tsx:88-104` writes a `broadcasts` document after a send with `stats: { sent, delivered, opened }`. `sent` and `delivered` are both `recipientIds.length` — a real count of the notification documents the code attempted to create (and did create, since `Promise.all` throwing would fail the whole broadcast rather than under-count). `opened` is `Math.round(recipientIds.length * 0.72)` — a hard-coded 72% conversion rate with no source. There is no open-tracking pixel, no delivery receipt, no read-status listener anywhere in this codebase (confirmed by grep for `opened`, `openRate`, `open rate` across `src/`, which finds only this line and the FIXME comment above it) — nothing measures whether any notification was ever opened. **Current blast radius is small but not zero**: a second grep for every reader of `stats.opened`/`.opened` across `src/` finds none — the same file's own broadcast-history list (`:227-238`) renders only `title`, `audience`, `message` and `status`, never `stats`. So today no admin sees the fabricated 72% in the product UI. The defect is that the number is persisted at all: it sits in the `broadcasts` document as if it were measured data, available to any future feature, data export, or admin reading the Firestore console directly, with nothing distinguishing it from a real metric — the same class of problem TRUST-2 and TRUST-4 closed for platform statistics and category job counts. A second grep for the same `Math.round(x.length * <fraction>)` shape and for other suspicious literal fractions near stat-looking fields (`0.6`–`0.8`) across `src/` finds nothing else matching this pattern — it is a one-off, not systemic. Fix: stop writing `opened` at all rather than writing a placeholder value (0, null, or a differently-labelled guess would still assert something false); `sent` and `delivered` are real and stay. Update the `BroadcastDoc` interface's `stats` shape to match what is actually written. Comment already present (`FIXME`) is resolved by the fix, not left in place restating a now-fixed problem. **Not touched, and explicitly out of scope**: any `broadcasts` document already written to `thenijobs-9f01d` under the old code path retains whatever fabricated `opened` value it was given at send time; correcting already-persisted production documents would be a live write against production data, which this phase does not do. This phase stops the fabrication going forward only. Expected files: `src/app/admin/notifications/page.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, any other admin page, `src/lib/firebase/firestoreService.ts`. Lanes: feedback, skill. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`6f72e87`) + this one; `ACTIVE` ledger rows = 0. Worktree: `/Users/saai_siddharth/Projects/Clients/Theni-Jobs-trust6`. Acceptance: `stats` written on send no longer includes `opened`; the `BroadcastDoc.stats` type matches; typecheck 0, build 0 with real credentials, lint no worse than the 2-error/683-warning baseline; page count checked before and after in this same worktree (a client-side field removal changes no route). **Not claimed:** a real open-rate measurement is not being added — there is no tracking mechanism to measure it, and building one is a separate, larger feature decision, not a bug fix. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `e5023a1`, fix `299d912`, merge `43fe7a4`. Gate after the last phase-branch commit: typecheck 0 · lint 1 (**2 errors / 683 warnings, unchanged from the standing baseline**) · **build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes** · secrets:bundle 0 · secrets:src-literals 0 · tracked-artefacts 0. **Revert-and-watch, in the phase worktree:** `git revert --no-commit 299d912` brought the `Math.round(recipientIds.length * 0.72)` line back (bug reproduced, confirmed by grep); `git revert --abort` restored the fix, `git status --porcelain` empty. **Page count checked, same worktree, before and after:** 572, unchanged — a dropped object field writes no new route and changes nothing the static export renders. Lanes: feedback PASS (the field was never surfaced to any user, so there is no user-facing message to correct — the fix is data-shape only), skill PASS. **Not verified:** no live read against `thenijobs-9f01d`'s `broadcasts` collection, before or after — the collection requires `isAuthenticated()` to read under `firestore.rules`, this session holds no admin credential, and the fix's correctness does not depend on what past documents contain. Any broadcast already sent under the old code path keeps its fabricated `opened` value; this phase does not and cannot correct already-persisted production documents without a write against production data, which is out of scope by rule. | `43fe7a4` (2026-09-05, inside `Theni-Jobs-develop`) |
| `thenijobs/sec4-admin-security-page-is-decorative` | `6e8492a` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEC-4 | **`admin/security/page.tsx` presents three security controls to the owner, and none of them do anything.** Found while sweeping unexplored admin pages after the TRUST-6 close-out. **(1) 'Two-factor auth' toggle** (`:271-281`), labelled 'Require 2FA for administrator login', defaults to **checked** (`useState(true)`, :121), is fully interactive, and writes `platformSettings/global.twoFa` on every toggle with a live 'Saving...' indicator (:234-239). A repository-wide grep for `multiFactor`, `MultiFactor`, `PhoneMultiFactorGenerator`, `TotpMultiFactorGenerator` finds **zero matches anywhere in `src/`** — this app never calls a single Firebase Multi-Factor Auth API. The toggle, and the boolean it persists, are read by nothing except this same page. An admin who visits this screen and sees the switch on has been told, by every visual signal a working control has (default-on state, live save indicator), that 2FA is required for admin login. It is not, and cannot be, since no MFA enrollment or verification code exists to enforce it. **(2) 'Session timeout' dropdown** (`:282-297`), labelled 'Auto-logout inactive sessions', same pattern: writes `platformSettings/global.sessionTimeout` (15/30/60 min), read by nothing else. A grep for `idle`, `inactiv`, `autoLogout`, `lastActivity` across `src/` turns up two matches, both false positives (`requestIdleCallback` in `AuthContext.tsx`, unrelated to session expiry). No inactivity timer, no forced sign-out, exists anywhere in the app. **(3) 'Role permission matrix'** (`:323-339`) is a hard-coded, non-interactive `PERMISSIONS` array asserting which of four roles — Super admin, Admin, Moderator, Support staff — may reach each of eight platform modules (e.g. row `reviews`: super_admin true, admin true, **moderator true**, support false). `src/lib/types/index.ts`'s real `UserRole` (the field actually stored on `users/{uid}.role`, checked by every Firestore rule's `isAdmin()` and by the admin layout guard `useRequireAuth(['admin', 'super_admin'])` at `admin/layout.tsx:39`) has **no `moderator` or `support` value at all** — those are drawn from a separate `AdminRole` type (`types/index.ts:16-22`) declared for a 'granular admin team roles' concept whose own carrying field, `adminRole?: AdminRole` (`types/index.ts:39`), is **never read or written anywhere in `src/`** — a grep for `adminRole` finds only its own type declaration. No user can hold a `moderator` or `support` role in this app today, and even if one somehow did, the admin route guard would refuse them entry before this table's distinctions could ever apply. The matrix describes a four-tier access system that was never built; the real system is binary — `admin`/`super_admin` get in and see everything under `/admin/**`, or a user is refused entry outright. **Not one of the three controls is a stale leftover of a removed feature** — none was ever wired to anything: no commit in this repository's history added, then later removed, an MFA call, an inactivity timer, or a granular-role check. This is UI built ahead of a backend that was never built to match it. Fix, following the same 'stop asserting something false' rule TRUST-2/3/4/6 already applied to public- and admin-facing statistics: remove the 'Security controls' card (2FA toggle, session-timeout dropdown, their state, handlers, and the now-dead `PlatformSettings` read) and the 'Role permission matrix' card (the hard-coded `PERMISSIONS` array, `Allowed` component, its column defs) entirely, rather than relabel them as 'coming soon' — a still-interactive-looking toggle that admits it does nothing is a stranger UI than removing it, and the two real cards on this page (the audit log and the authorised-admin roster, both genuinely Firestore-backed) already carry the page's real content. Update the page's header description to match what remains. Expected files: `src/app/admin/security/page.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `AuthContext.tsx`, `useAuth.ts`, `src/lib/types/index.ts` (the unused `AdminRole`/`adminRole` type stays as-is — removing a type declaration is a separate, smaller cleanup this phase does not fold in, since nothing depends on it either way and touching shared types is out of proportion to a single page's fix), any other `src/app/admin/**` page. Rebuilding the untouched `Theni-Jobs-develop` worktree at that exact same commit a second time, with zero source changes from either phase, **also returned 575** — proving the number moved because live production Firestore data changed between the two build runs (this repository's `generateStaticParams` routes read real companies/jobs/portfolio sites), not because of anything in this diff or TRUST-6's. | `thenijobs/trust7-fabricated-gst-tax-invoice` | `094d849` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-7 | **An admin action generates a downloadable PDF titled 'TAX INVOICE' carrying a fabricated GSTIN, a fabricated registered address, and a fabricated corporate designation, for real paying subscribers.** The most severe finding of this session so far — not a UI trust issue but a document with real legal/compliance exposure. Found immediately after OPS-1 while sweeping remaining admin pages for the settings/pattern audit. `admin/subscriptions/page.tsx:63-150` `handleDownloadGSTInvoice()`, wired to a real 'Download GST tax invoice PDF' button (`:276-277`) against real Firestore `subscriptions` documents (`useCollection<SubscriptionDoc>('subscriptions')`, `:42`), generates a jsPDF document with: title **'TAX INVOICE'**; company name **'THENIJOBS DIGITAL PRIVATE LIMITED'**; **'Reg Office: NRT Road, Theni District, Tamil Nadu - 625531'**; **'GSTIN: 33AAAAA0000A1Z5'**; a computed CGST/SGST 9%/9% tax breakdown reverse-engineered from the paid amount (`baseAmount = amount / 1.18`); an HSN/SAC code (`998314`); and the boilerplate line 'This is a computer-generated tax invoice and requires no physical signature' — every visual and textual signal of an authentic, legally-issued GST tax invoice. **Every one of the specific claimed facts is fabricated, and the internal evidence is unambiguous — this is not a guess.** The GSTIN `33AAAAA0000A1Z5` is the exact same string used elsewhere in this codebase purely as an input-format example (`admin/businesses/page.tsx:1028`, `placeholder="e.g. 33AAAAA0000A1Z5"` — a generic sample GSTIN shown to illustrate the expected format for OTHER businesses' own GST numbers, not a real registered number). 'NRT Road' appears elsewhere only as a placeholder example address (`employer/post-job/page.tsx:655`, `placeholder="e.g. 45, NRT Road, Opp. Bus Stand, Theni"`); '625531' is a generic fallback postal code used repeatedly across the app as a default when no real value is supplied (`src/app/page.tsx:111`, `src/lib/seo/jobSchema.ts:105`, `src/lib/excel/companyExcelService.ts:672,800`) — not a specific registered office. **A real, different, already-used-elsewhere address and support email already exist**: `src/lib/constants.ts`'s `SITE_CONTACT` exports `fullAddress: 'North Street, A.M. Patty, Uthamapalayam, Theni District, Tamil Nadu - 625533, India'` and `supportEmail: 'support@thenijobs.com'` — a different street, a different postal code (625533 vs the invoice's fabricated 625531), proving the invoice's address was assembled from unrelated placeholder fragments rather than sourced from the one real address constant this app already has. Whether 'THENIJOBS DIGITAL PRIVATE LIMITED' is the real registered legal entity name, and whether the business holds a real GSTIN at all, cannot be determined from the repository and is not assumed either way. **Why this outranks every other finding this session:** TRUST-1 through TRUST-6 and SEC-4 were UI trust/security-theater issues — a wrong number, a switch that does nothing. This generates a document a real paying employer could rely on for their own GST input-tax-credit filing, or that could constitute issuing an invoice with incorrect or fictitious GSTIN/registration details — a matter with real regulatory exposure under Indian GST law, not just a misleading dashboard. This is the same class of harm CLAUDE.md's rule 7 and the recent commit `6f34dc3 fix: stop fabricating businesses, repair job approval and production routing` already exist to prevent, applied here to a legal document instead of a business listing. Fix: convert the generated PDF from a GST tax invoice to an honest payment receipt. Remove the fabricated GSTIN line, the fabricated registered-office address, the 'DIGITAL PRIVATE LIMITED' corporate designation, the CGST/SGST tax breakdown, and the HSN/SAC code entirely — none of it can be legally asserted without owner-supplied real values this session cannot obtain or verify. Replace the fabricated address/support line with the real `SITE_CONTACT.fullAddress`/`SITE_CONTACT.supportEmail` already used elsewhere in the app. Retitle 'TAX INVOICE' to 'PAYMENT RECEIPT', 'Invoice No/Date' to 'Receipt No/Date', and state explicitly in the footer that this is a payment receipt, not a GST tax invoice — the same explicit-disclosure pattern this file's own 'Franchise hub' tab (a different admin page) already uses for its placeholder data. Show the amount paid as a single line with no invented tax split. Rename the handler and button label/title to match. Expected files: `src/app/admin/subscriptions/page.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `src/lib/constants.ts` (read-only import of the existing `SITE_CONTACT` export), payment routes, any other admin page. Lanes: uiux, feedback, skill. **Not a security-rules or Firestore-write change** — this is a pure client-side PDF-generation fix; no Firestore document shape changes. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`4de3d78`) + this one; `ACTIVE` rows = 0. Worktree: `/Users/saai_siddharth/Projects/Clients/Theni-Jobs-trust7`. Acceptance: no GSTIN, no fabricated address, no 'PRIVATE LIMITED', no CGST/SGST breakdown, no HSN/SAC code remain anywhere in the generated PDF's text calls; the real `SITE_CONTACT` address/email are used instead; the document is explicitly labelled a payment receipt, not a tax invoice; typecheck 0, build 0 with real credentials, lint no worse than the 2-error/683-warning baseline. **Not claimed:** this phase does not determine or assert the real legal status of the business (registered company vs. sole proprietorship) or whether it holds any GST registration at all — it removes claims this session cannot verify rather than substituting a different unverified claim. If the owner has a real GSTIN and wants genuine GST-compliant invoicing, that is a follow-on decision requiring owner-supplied registration details, not something this phase can supply. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `404f356`, fix `094d849`, merge `1c5f20d`. Gate after the last phase-branch commit: typecheck 0 · lint 1 (**2 errors / 683 warnings, unchanged baseline, neither in the touched file**) · **build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes** · secrets:bundle 0 · secrets:src-literals 0 · tracked-artefacts 0. **Revert-and-watch, in the phase worktree:** `git revert --no-commit 094d849` brought the GSTIN, the fabricated address, the 'PRIVATE LIMITED' designation and the CGST/SGST breakdown back (4 matches by grep); `git revert --abort` restored the fix, `git status --porcelain` empty. **Every fabricated fact confirmed against internal evidence, not asserted from outside knowledge:** the GSTIN string is reused verbatim elsewhere in this codebase as a generic input-placeholder example; the address fragments (street name, postal code) each independently trace to unrelated placeholder/fallback strings used elsewhere; the real, different `SITE_CONTACT` address and postal code already exist and are now what the receipt uses instead. Lanes: uiux PASS, feedback PASS (the disclosure line added to the footer — 'not a GST tax invoice' — follows the same explicit-disclosure pattern this same file's own Franchise hub tab already uses for its placeholder data), skill PASS. **Not verified:** no rendered-PDF text extraction was run to confirm the generated document's actual visual layout — verification relied on `tsc`'s type-check of the exact string literals passed to `pdf.text()`, a full grep confirming no fabricated identifier survives anywhere in the file, and the successful static build; jsPDF's layout/rendering itself is unchanged library behavior, only the string content passed to it changed. **Severity note carried to the standing ledger record, not just this row:** this is assessed as the most consequential finding of the whole `fix-2026-09` programme to date — real regulatory/legal exposure from a document format (GST tax invoicing) rather than a UI trust or security issue, which is why it was fixed immediately rather than logged as BLOCKED the way OPS-1 (found minutes earlier, same sweep) was — OPS-1's right answer depends on unknowable owner product intent across five different subsystems; this one's right answer (stop asserting unverifiable legal/tax facts) holds regardless of what the owner ultimately wants to build. | `1c5f20d` (2026-09-05, inside `Theni-Jobs-develop`) |

Verified Candidate' pass, and no verification of any kind gates the label.** Found sweeping other `jsPDF` call sites after TRUST-7's fabricated invoice. `src/components/id-card/SeekerIDCard.tsx`'s `SeekerIDCardProps.seeker` interface (`:9-24`) carries no `isVerified` field or anything like one, yet the component unconditionally prints 'Verified Candidate' on: the visible on-screen badge (`:181`, `Verified Candidate · THENIJOBS`); the downloadable PDF pass (`:115`, `'THENIJOBS — Official Digital Candidate Pass'`, and `:119`, `... • Verified Candidate`); and the WhatsApp share message (`:146`, `Verified THENIJOBS Candidate Pass` / `live verified portfolio & CV`). `src/app/seeker/id-card/page.tsx` repeats the same claim in its own copy (`:66` 'Your official THENIJOBS verified candidate identity card', `:93` 'verified candidate pass', `:106` 'verified qualifications'). Every one of these strings is a compile-time literal — there is no runtime check anywhere in either file gating 'Verified' on any actual verification event (no phone-OTP-linked flag, no admin review, no document check).
