| `thenijobs/doc2-2-maintenance-mode` | `565d2f4` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("continue the doc2-production-audit-2026-09 loop autonomously", 2026-09-06, via `/thenijobs run` loop tick, phase DOC2-2) | **The one item DOC2-1 deliberately left untouched**: `maintenance` mode, the single highest-blast-radius toggle on the admin settings page — it can gate the *entire site* for *every visitor* if built with any bug, unlike the three narrow feature killswitches DOC2-1 already shipped. Designed carefully before writing code, per the phase's own stated requirements: **(1) fail-safe default** — a missing `platformSettings/public.maintenance` field, a still-loading read, or a read error must always mean 'site is up', never 'site is down'; the gate only activates on an explicit `=== true`, exactly mirroring the fail-open shape DOC2-1's three toggles already established (`!== false` there; `=== true` here — same principle, inverted because this flag defaults to absent-means-off rather than absent-means-on). **(2) Admins are never blocked**: verified `src/app/layout.tsx`'s actual structure first — a Server Component wrapping `<AuthProvider><NotificationProvider><ToastProvider>{children}</ToastProvider></NotificationProvider></AuthProvider>` — and added a new client wrapper, `MaintenanceGate`, around `{children}` there. It reads the current path via `usePathname()` and unconditionally passes every `/admin/**` and `/login` route straight through, never evaluating the maintenance flag for them at all — an admin can always reach the login page and their own dashboard to flip it back off, and the *existing* per-page `useRequireAuth(['admin','super_admin'])` guards already correctly keep non-admins out of `/admin/**` regardless of maintenance state, so exempting the route prefix (not checking a role here) is sufficient and does not open any new access path. **(3) A confirmation step** was added specifically for turning maintenance ON (not off, which is always safe) — a plain `window.confirm()` before the toggle fires, matching the existing `confirm()` pattern already used for destructive actions elsewhere in this codebase (e.g. SEEKER-7's section removal), rather than a single accidental click taking the whole site down. **Mechanism**: reuses DOC2-1's own `platformSettings/public` document and dual-write pattern exactly — `maintenance` is added alongside `features`, written by the same admin settings page, read by the same already-public rule block (no `firestore.rules` change needed, since DOC2-1's `allow read: if true` already covers the whole document, not just the `features` field). Non-admin, non-login routes show a plain 'under maintenance' page instead of their real content when the flag resolves to explicitly `true`; while the read is still loading, real content renders normally (briefly showing then potentially replacing content is a rare, minor cost for an action admins take deliberately and infrequently — the alternative, a loading spinner on every single page load site-wide to avoid it, is a much worse and far more frequent UX cost for a flag that is almost always false). Expected files: `src/app/admin/settings/page.tsx` (add `maintenance` to the existing dual-write, add the confirm-before-enabling step), new `src/components/system/MaintenanceGate.tsx`, `src/app/layout.tsx` (wrap `{children}` with it), this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules` (already covered by DOC2-1's `public` block), any `/admin/**` page's own auth guard (unchanged, still the actual access-control mechanism for who reaches admin content), or the three feature killswitches DOC2-1 already built (untouched). Built instead: a **bounded, client-side** search — a new `AdminQuickSearch.tsx` component fetches the 200 most recent (`orderBy('createdAt','desc'), limit(200)`) documents from each of `users`/`companies`/`jobs` once per session (cached in component state, not refetched per keystroke), then filters client-side on every keystroke by a case-insensitive substring match against `displayName`/`name`/`email` (users), `name` (companies), and `title`/`companyName` (jobs) — a real, useful search over the most-recently-active records, explicitly NOT a claim of searching the platform's full history, since older records outside the most recent 200 per collection are not reachable this way (disclosed, not hidden). Results are grouped by type (max 5 shown per group) in a dropdown beneath the input; clicking one navigates to that entity's own admin list page with a `?q=<value>` parameter. **The three target list pages did not previously read any URL search parameter** — added a small, additive `useSearchParams().get('q')` read on mount to seed each page's own already-working local `searchQuery` state (`admin/users/page.tsx`, `admin/businesses/page.tsx`, `admin/jobs/page.tsx`), so a quick-search click lands the admin on a page already filtered to the match, using each page's own existing, unmodified filter logic — no new filter logic duplicated. Expected files: new `src/components/admin/AdminQuickSearch.tsx`, `src/app/admin/layout.tsx` (swap the dead input for the new component), `src/app/admin/users/page.tsx`, `src/app/admin/businesses/page.tsx`, `src/app/admin/jobs/page.tsx` (each: one small additive `useEffect` reading `?q=`), this ledger. Never touches any other admin page, the `activityLogs`/audit-trail feature (already correct), the business bulk-import feature (already correct, narrower gap not pursued), or any collection's actual data. | `thenijobs/employer1-fix-portfolio-plan-field-and-tier-enforcement` | `e3bd0ee` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("continue the loop into Employer platform", 2026-09-06) via the doc2-production-audit-2026-09 programme's Employer-platform-enhancements area | **Two coupled defects found investigating Doc 2's "company website tiers" note fresh against the repository.** (1) A field-name bug: `src/app/employer/website/templates/page.tsx:52`, `editor/page.tsx:43` and `settings/page.tsx:191` all read `company?.planSlug`, but no `companies` document anywhere in this codebase ever writes a `planSlug` field — the real, billing-relevant field is `subscriptionPlan` (set at successful payment in `src/app/api/payment/verify/route.ts:247,266` and by `src/lib/firebase/bulkCompanyService.ts:234`; correctly read as `company?.subscriptionPlan` already in `post-job/page.tsx:80`, `website/page.tsx:43` and `company-profile/page.tsx:411` etc). Because `planSlug` is always `undefined`, `const planSlug = company?.planSlug || 'free'` always evaluates to `'free'` regardless of a company's real paid tier — every standard/premium/enterprise subscriber sees their own portfolio template picker, section picker and digital-visiting-card plan badge as if they had never upgraded, and cannot select the templates or sections they already paid for. (2) A related server-side gap, not just a display bug: `firestore.rules:427-451`'s `portfolioSites` `allow create`/`allow update` rules check only `ownerId`/`isAdmin()` — nothing cross-checks the written `templateId` against the owner's real plan. `handleSelectTemplate` in `templates/page.tsx:56-119` writes `templateId` directly via `createDocument`/`updateDocument` after only a client-side `canAccessTemplate` gate (`src/lib/plans.ts:80`); a free-tier company can bypass that gate with a direct SDK/devtools write and grant itself any of the 11 non-free templates (`TEMPLATE_PLAN_ACCESS`, `src/lib/constants.ts:642-647`), with nothing in the database stopping it. Fix: (a) correct the three read sites to `company?.subscriptionPlan`; (b) add `planRank`, `templateMinPlan` and `portfolioTemplateAllowedForOwner` helper functions to `firestore.rules` (mirroring `PORTFOLIO_TEMPLATES`' per-template `plan` tiers, since rules cannot import a TS constant) and wire them into `portfolioSites`' `create` and `update` rules, gated to `ownerType == 'company'` only (seeker portfolios stay untouched, per `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx:161`'s existing hardcoded `planSlug: 'free'` design) and bypassed for `isAdmin()` and for updates that do not change `templateId`, matching every other admin-override precedent in this ruleset. Expected files: `src/app/employer/website/templates/page.tsx`, `src/app/employer/website/editor/page.tsx`, `src/app/employer/website/settings/page.tsx`, `firestore.rules`, this ledger. Never touches `SeekerSiteEditor.tsx`, `src/lib/types/portfolio.ts`, `src/lib/constants.ts`, `src/lib/plans.ts`, any payment/subscriptionPlan-writing code, or the `portfolioSites.planSlug` document field itself (unread anywhere per a repository-wide grep — dead, but out of scope; it will start reflecting the real plan anyway once the source variable is fixed, at no extra cost). | `thenijobs/msg1-wire-real-read-receipts` | `468df38` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("continue the loop into Messaging", 2026-09-06) via the doc2-production-audit-2026-09 programme's Messaging area | **A fabricated read-receipt indicator, same severity class as this session's own TRUST-13/TRUST-14/LEADS-1 findings.** Investigating Messaging fresh (both `seeker/messages/page.tsx` and `employer/messages/page.tsx` were confirmed genuinely real for the core chat flow — correct `participants`-scoped/`companyId`-scoped queries matching `firestore.rules`' already-hardened `conversations`/`conversations/{chatId}/messages` rules from a prior SEC-2/FIX-1/FIX-2 pass, real `onSnapshot` listeners, real sends). But `employer/messages/page.tsx:357` renders `{isMe && <CheckCheck size={11} />}` — a WhatsApp-style blue double-checkmark 'read' indicator — **unconditionally on every message the employer has ever sent**, regardless of whether the candidate has actually opened the conversation. A repository-wide grep for `read`/`isRead`/`readAt`/`deliveredAt`/`seenAt` anywhere near the messages subcollection returns zero matches: there is no read-tracking data anywhere in this codebase. Every employer sees every message they send as 'read' the instant it sends — including interview invites and offer discussions (this page's own `QUICK_TEMPLATES`) — which can mislead an employer into believing a candidate saw and ignored them when the candidate may never have opened the app. The `Check` (single-check) icon is imported at line 13 but never rendered anywhere — confirming no 'sent but unread' state was ever wired, only the false 'read' one. Also found `Conversation.unreadCount` (`:50`) declared but never set or read anywhere in the file — genuinely dead, unlike the checkmark it declares nothing false, just unused. Fix: **build a real read-receipt**, not remove the display (unlike ADS-1, a correct backing implementation is small and low-risk here). `firestore.rules`' existing `conversations` `allow update` rule already permits a participant/seeker/company-owner to write any field except the five membership fields `conversationMembershipUnchanged()` protects — verified by re-reading `firestore.rules:494-524` — so a new `lastReadAt.<uid>` map entry needs **no rules change**. `seeker/messages/page.tsx`'s message-listener effect will write `lastReadAt.<seekerUid>: serverTimestamp()` on the conversation whenever the seeker's view of it refreshes (covers both 'opened' and 'new message arrived while open'). `employer/messages/page.tsx` will compare each own-sent message's timestamp against `activeConv.lastReadAt?.[otherUserId]` to choose `Check` (sent, not yet confirmed read) vs `CheckCheck` (read) — failing toward the safe direction (single check) whenever the field is missing, in-flight, or ambiguous, the opposite bias from today's always-double-check bug. Also fixes a related staleness gap this introduces if left alone: the employer page's `activeConv` is a frozen local snapshot taken at click time, not a live reference into the real-time `conversations` list, so without an added re-sync effect the read receipt would never update while a chat stays open — added a small effect keyed on `conversations` that refreshes `activeConv` by id via the functional `setState` form (no new dependency-array entry needed). Removes the dead `unreadCount` field from the `Conversation` interface as a same-file, same-class, one-line cleanup. Expected files: `src/app/seeker/messages/page.tsx`, `src/app/employer/messages/page.tsx`, this ledger. Never touches `firestore.rules` (verified unnecessary above), `firestoreService.ts`'s `applyToJob`, any other conversations consumer (grepped repo-wide: none exist outside these two files). Lanes: uiux, feedback, skill. **Collision check at claim:** `git worktree list` = primary (`main`) + `Theni-Jobs-develop` (`3815d9b`) + this one; no unmerged/ACTIVE row overlapping `messages` or `conversations`. | `thenijobs/bill1-fix-broken-featured-job-checkbox` | `6d72293` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("Continue", 2026-09-06) via the doc2-production-audit-2026-09 programme's Billing area | **A visible, normal-looking checkbox on the job-posting form silently breaks the entire job posting whenever it's checked, for every employer on every plan.** Investigating Billing & Subscriptions fresh (PAYMENT-1/CREDIT-1 remain the only backend-blocked items; PAY-1's price mismatch, PAY-2's job-limit enforcement, and TRUST-7's fabricated GST invoice were already fixed by prior phases — re-verified, not re-done) surfaced a new, previously undiscovered functional bug while checking whether 'Featured Search Priority' (a Premium/Enterprise pricing-page perk, `src/lib/constants.ts:110,146`) actually works for anyone. `employer/post-job/page.tsx`'s 'Promotion Badges' section (`:588-591`) offers every employer, regardless of plan, a '⭐ Featured Job Opportunity' checkbox that sets `isFeatured: true` in the create payload (`:196`). But `firestore.rules`' `safeJobCreate()` (`:47-55`) unconditionally requires `(!d.keys().hasAny(['isFeatured']) || d.isFeatured == false)` on every job create — checking that box makes the ENTIRE job-posting write violate the rule and get rejected outright with a Firestore permission-denied error, not just the flag being ignored. The catch block (`:249-251`) correctly surfaces this as an honest `toast.error` rather than a fabricated success (no TRUST-class finding here), but the employer sees a generic 'Please check your inputs and try again' message that gives no indication the checkbox they just ticked is what broke the submission. Confirmed this isn't a missing feature but an already-built, already-working ADMIN-only mechanism being duplicated badly on the employer side: `admin/jobs/page.tsx`'s real `doToggleFeatured()` (`:199-203`) already lets an admin mark any approved job Featured post-creation via `updateDocument`, and `jobs`' own `allow update` rule (`:224-227`) correctly permits this only for `isAdmin()` — an employer's own update is allowed only when `jobModerationUnchanged()` holds, which explicitly protects `isFeatured` (`firestore.rules:104-108`) from employer self-service changes even after creation. The feature was deliberately designed as admin-editorial, matching the same pattern already used for `status`/`isActive`/`rejectionReason` on this exact rule — the employer-facing checkbox was simply never wired to respect that, and has apparently never worked since whenever it was added. Fix: remove the broken, always-failing checkbox and its live preview branch from `employer/post-job/page.tsx` — `isUrgent`/`isPremium` (unaffected by `safeJobCreate()`, confirmed by the same rule read) stay exactly as they are, this only removes the one option that can never work. Does not touch `firestore.rules`, `admin/jobs/page.tsx`, or the real featured-toggle mechanism, which is correct as-is. Expected files: `src/app/employer/post-job/page.tsx`, this ledger. **Separately flagged to the owner, not acted on in this phase:** several pricing-page marketing bullets (`src/lib/constants.ts`) — 'Google Analytics & Meta Pixel', 'Advanced Analytics & Leads Dashboard' / 'Advanced analytics', 'Custom Domain Support', '24×7 Priority Support' — have zero implementation anywhere in the codebase (grepped repo-wide), and even 'Featured Search Priority' itself, once this bug is fixed, remains editorial-only with no plan-based linkage (`doToggleFeatured` doesn't check the poster's plan). Removing paid-tier sales copy is a pricing/business decision, not a pure wiring call, so it was not acted on autonomously under the standing 'skip backend-dependent items, build the rest' authorization, which was scoped to decorative UI, not sales messaging. | `thenijobs/errors1-fix-fabricated-status-and-hydration-bug` | `932e49e` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | owner ("Fix all the 84 errors and the AI", 2026-09-06, after screenshotting the live production admin panel: AI Settings' Test button crashing, and the Error Monitoring dashboard showing 84 tracked errors) | **Investigated every distinct error class shown live in production before touching anything — 84 logged entries resolve to a handful of real causes, not 84 distinct bugs.** (1) `admin/errors/page.tsx`'s 'AI Engine (Groq/Gemini)' stat card is fabricated: `loadData()` (`:82-83`) unconditionally sets `aiApiStatus` to `'connected'` on every successful page load regardless of the AI backend's real state, and the JSX itself (`:267`) doesn't even read that variable — 'Connected • High Speed' is a hardcoded string, not reactive to anything. This is why the dashboard claimed the AI was healthy in the exact same screenshot session where the AI Settings Test button was failing. (2) That Test failure (`admin/ai-settings/page.tsx:125`, `const result = await response.json();`) crashes with 'Unexpected token <' because `/api/ai/test` returns Vercel's generic 404 HTML page in this static-export production deployment — confirmed architectural, the same class as PAYMENT-1/CREDIT-1 (no backend exists to serve `src/app/api/**` routes in production; they only run under `next dev`). Not fixable without a real backend migration — out of scope, matching the owner's own prior 'skip backend-dependent items' authorization. (3) Cross-checked every distinct error MESSAGE in the visible entries: the 'Cannot read properties of null (reading \'removeChild\')' errors hit `/services`, `/businesses`, `/agrimore` (×2) and two unrelated `/company/*` pages, every one with a blank 'Component' field — the signature of `src/lib/firebase/errorTracker.ts`'s blanket `window.addEventListener('error', ...)` handler logging EVERY uncaught error indiscriminately, including ones caused by browser extensions (Google Translate is the textbook cause of exactly this message) mutating the DOM outside React's control — not a shared app bug; grepped the whole `src/` tree for manual `appendChild`/`removeChild`/`createElement` usage and found nothing that touches these five unrelated pages. (4) Two missing Firestore composite indexes, confirmed by the live console errors: `jobs` (`status` ASC + `createdAt` DESC, used by `admin/dashboard/page.tsx:137-140`'s pending-jobs widget) has no definition at all in `firestore.indexes.json`; `errors` (`status` ASC + `lastOccurred` DESC, `errorService.ts:194-207`) already has a definition in the file (`:199-205`) but was evidently never deployed — this session cannot run `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes`, printed for the owner only. (5) A hydration bug **this session introduced today**, in ADMIN-QS-1: `admin/jobs/page.tsx:97-99`, `admin/users/page.tsx:64`, `admin/businesses/page.tsx:101` all initialize `searchQuery` via `useState(() => typeof window === 'undefined' ? '' : new URLSearchParams(...).get('q') || '')` — at static-build time `typeof window === 'undefined'` is always true (empty string baked into the shell), but the first real client render (hydration) evaluates the same initializer with a real `window`, returning a non-empty value whenever a `?q=` param is present (exactly what `AdminQuickSearch`'s own `router.push` produces) — a guaranteed hydration mismatch on that exact navigation path. The other four hydration-error entries (`admin/users/create`, `admin/errors`, `seeker/dashboard`, `profile`) are all dated 8/29 — checked each file for the same window/localStorage/Date.now/Math.random patterns and found none; not reproduced, not fixed, flagged as unresolved rather than guessed at. Fix: (1) wire a real `aiApiStatus` check (a lightweight `fetch('/api/ai/test', {method:'HEAD'})`, `status === 404` → 'degraded', anything else → 'connected') and make the JSX read it instead of a hardcoded string; (2) in `handleTest`, check the response is actually JSON before parsing and show a clear 'AI testing isn't available on this deployment' message instead of the raw parse-error text; (3) add the missing `jobs` composite index to `firestore.indexes.json`; (4) change the three `searchQuery` initializers to start at `''` unconditionally and set the real value from a post-hydration `useEffect`, closing the mismatch window entirely; (5) add a small allowlist filter to `errorTracker.ts`'s `handleError` that skips known browser-extension-noise signatures (`removeChild`/`insertBefore`/`appendChild` 'null' messages with no `event.error` object) before logging them as app defects. **Deliberately not touched, flagged only**: `storageStatus`'s fabricated '99.9% Quota' claim and the static 'Security & Auth: Rules Active • Protected' text in the same file — same fabrication class, found in passing, not fixed here because a reliable client-side Storage-quota or rules-health check isn't feasible within this pass's scope; the four stale (8/29) hydration entries with no current reproducing code found. Expected files: `src/app/admin/errors/page.tsx`, `src/app/admin/ai-settings/page.tsx`, `firestore.indexes.json`, `src/app/admin/jobs/page.tsx`, `src/app/admin/users/page.tsx`, `src/app/admin/businesses/page.tsx`, `src/lib/firebase/errorTracker.ts`, this ledger. Never touches `src/app/api/ai/**` (would require a real backend, out of scope), `storageStatus`, the Security & Auth card. Worktree: `/Users/saai_siddharth/Projects/Clients/Theni-Jobs-seekerredesign1`. `npm ci` done (warnings only, matches this repo's known baseline). Identity confirmed: `git config user.name/user.email` = `SRIESWARAN01 <srieswaran01@users.noreply.github.com>`. Gate baseline at claim (`78d4138`): typecheck 0 (9s) · lint 0 (15s) · build 0 (29s) with real-firebase-config=yes (3/3). **Research finding — a real, verifiable bug, not a cosmetic gap:** read `seeker/dashboard/page.tsx` (390 lines) and `seeker/profile/page.tsx` (629 lines) in full. `profile/page.tsx`'s own save handler (`handleSaveProfile`) writes the seeker's full profile (education, experience, skills, photo, address, currentRole, etc.) to `seekerProfiles/{uid}`, and syncs only `displayName`/`email`/`phone`/`district` back to `users/{uid}` — `resume/page.tsx` independently confirms the same split (resumes live in `seekerProfiles/{uid}.resumes[]`, never on `users`). But `dashboard/page.tsx`'s "Profile strength" widget computed every check (`photo`, `skills`, `education`, `experience`, `resume`, `bio`) by reading `useAuth()`'s `user` object, which is backed by `users/{uid}` — fields that document structurally never receives. This is not hypothetical: it means a seeker who fully completes their profile via `/seeker/profile` — photo uploaded, 5 skills added, 2 jobs of experience, a resume uploaded — would see the dashboard tell them their profile is still missing a photo, skills, experience, and a resume, forever, because those checks were reading the wrong document. The dashboard's own `SeekerProfile` interface even carried a `bio`/`about` field with no corresponding input anywhere in `profile/page.tsx` — a checklist item ("Write a short bio") that could never be satisfied by any code path. Separately, `profile/page.tsx`'s own strength widget (`strengthItems`, computed correctly from its own local state since it's the page doing the writing) checked a different 8-item set than the dashboard's 8-item set, with no overlap on 4 of them (name, address, career details/current role, and resume were on neither or only one side) — meaning the SAME seeker saw two different, disagreeing "profile strength" percentages depending which screen they were on. **Built 2026-09-08** (1 commit, `cd6430f`). Build confirmed the intended effect directly: all 7 `src/app/api/**` routes now show as `ƒ` (dynamic, server-rendered) in the build manifest instead of being silently absent from a static export, and no `out/` directory is produced at all. `package-lock.json`: `firebase-admin`'s dependency tree introduces 6 moderate transitive advisories (`@google-cloud/storage`/`gaxios`/`retry-request`/`teeny-request`/`uuid`, `npm audit fix --dry-run` confirms no fix available without a breaking change) — a deliberate, documented tradeoff: Google's own official Admin SDK, needed to close a WORSE, already-real vulnerability (unauthenticated writes denied by the default-deny rules). The pre-existing `xlsx` high-severity advisory is confirmed unrelated (already present in `package-lock.json` before this commit, `git show HEAD~1:package-lock.json \| grep -c '"xlsx"'` = 2). **A real regression found and fixed live in `e972a4b`, not anticipated in the claim:** `75995e3` hoisted `const db = getAdminFirestore();` at the top of `payment/verify/route.ts`'s `POST` handler, before the signature/plan/amount validation checks. Since `getAdminFirestore()` throws synchronously when uncredentialed (the actual state of this environment), EVERY request — including ones that should have been correctly refused with a specific 400/403 — instead fell into the generic 500 "could not be recorded" catch-all. Caught by live-testing the real dev-server route with `curl`, not by reading the diff: a request with no `paymentId`/`signature` returned `500` instead of the intended `403 "Refusing to activate..."`. Fixed in `e972a4b` by only calling `getAdminFirestore()` inside the branches that actually write: the two best-effort audit-log writes (`status:'failed'`, signature-mismatch) now go through a small `bestEffortWrite()` helper that catches a *synchronous* throw from an uncredentialed Admin SDK (a bare `.catch()` on the write promise does not catch a throw that happens before any promise exists) without blocking that branch's own intended response; the main success-path write only calls `getAdminFirestore()` after every validation check has already passed, so a missing credential there is now a genuine, correctly-reported write failure, not a false positive for an otherwise-valid refusal.
